Coulomb Gauges and Regularity for Stationary Weak Yang–Mills Connections in Supercritical Dimension
Riccardo Caniato, Tristan Rivière

Abstract
. We prove that stationary Yang–Mills fields in dimensions 5 belonging to the variational class of weak connections are smooth away from a closed singular set S S of vanishing 1-dimensional Hausdorff measure. Our proof is based on an ε \varepsilon -regularity theorem, which generalizes to this class of weak connections the existing previous ε \varepsilon -regularity results by G. Tian for smooth connections, by Y. Meyer and the second author for Sobolev and approximable connections, and by T. Tao and G. Tian for admissible connections (which are weak limits of smooth Yang–Mills fields). On the path towards establishing ε \varepsilon -regularity, a pivotal step is the construction of controlled Coulomb gauges for general weak connections under small Morrey norm assumptions.



MSC (2020): 58E15 (primary)
; 49Q15, 49Q20, 53C65, 81T13 (secondary).

I.
Introduction

The study of the variations of the Yang–Mills Lagrangian has known a spectacular development since the very first analytical works by K. Uhlenbeck, which have been central in producing new invariants of differential structures on topological
4
-manifolds. In particular, S. Donaldson proved his celebrated result on the existence of non-smoothable topological
4
-manifolds by studying properties of the the moduli space of the anti-self-dual instantons
1
1

1

Instantons in dimension 4 are very special critical points of the Yang–Mills functional solving a first order PDE, in a similar fashion as holomorphic functions are very special critical points of the Dirichlet energy in dimension 2.
over such manifolds (see
[
7
]
,
[
8
]
). Examples of these manifolds had already been constructed by Freedman in
[
12
]
. Moreover, Taubes proved the existence of uncountably many fake
\mathbb{R}^{4}
’s by means of gauge theoretic methods in
[
32
]
. For a complete discussion of these topics, we refer the reader to
[
9
]
or
[
11
]
.

Due to the successful use of the Yang–Mills energy in dimension 4, it is natural to explore its behavior in higher dimensions, specifically in the
supercritical
regime. In fact, S. Donaldson and R. Thomas outlined a research program in this direction in
[
10
]
,
[
33
]
. However, analyzing the Yang–Mills Lagrangian becomes increasingly challenging in dimensions higher than 4, where we are led to consider
singular
solutions, which naturally emerge in this more complicated context.

Locally, the Yang–Mills Lagrangian is defined as follows. Let
A
be a 1-form on the flat
n
-dimensional unit ball
{\mathbb{B}}^{n}
taking values into the Lie Algebra
\mathfrak{g}
of a compact matrix Lie group
G
. The
Yang–Mills energy
of this “connection
1
-form” is given by

(I.1)

\displaystyle\mbox{YM}(A):=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|dA+A\wedge A|^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{n},

where
\mathcal{L}^{n}
denotes the
Lebesgue measure
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
A\wedge A
is the
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 2-form given by

(I.2)

\displaystyle A\wedge A(X,Y):=[A(X),A(Y)]\qquad\forall\,X,Y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

is the Lie Algebra bracket on
\mathfrak{g}
. The curvature
F_{A}:=dA+A\wedge A
is the sum of a
linear operation
on
A
(i.e.
\,dA
) and a
bilinear
one (i.e.
A\wedge A
). One of the main difficulties in the analysis of the Yang–Mills functional is to understand which one of the two is “taking over” the other along sequences with uniformly bounded Yang–Mills energy. As we will see shortly, the general answer to this question strongly depends on the dimension
n
of the base manifold.

The Yang–Mills Lagrangian is conformally invariant in dimension 4. This makes the 4-dimensional case critical from a purely analytic perspective, as we are explaining in the forthcoming paragraphs. The Yang–Mills Lagrangian is invariant under the following
gauge change operation
:

(I.3)

\displaystyle\mbox{YM}(A^{g})=\mbox{YM}(A),\quad\mbox{ where }\quad A^{g}:=g^{-1}\,dg+g^{-1}\,A\,g

for any choice of map
g
from
\mathbb{B}^{n}
into
G
. These maps are called
local gauge transformations
or simply
local gauges
and they realize the so called
local gauge group
. The equality between the Yang–Mills energy of
A
and the Yang–Mills energy of
A^{g}
is a direct consequence of the following identity
2
2

2

The action of the local gauge group on a given connection — i.e. an equivariant horizontal plane distribution in the principal
G
-bundle which is represented in a local trivialization by a
\mathfrak{g}
valued 1-forms
A
in the base — is converted into the adjoint action of
G
on
\mathfrak{g}
at the level of the curvature which is itself measuring the lack of integrability of this plane distribution (recall that the curvature is the vertical projection of the bracket of horizontal lifts of vector fields in the base).

(I.4)

\displaystyle F_{A^{g}}=g^{-1}F_{A}g\quad\Rightarrow\quad|F_{A^{g}}|=|F_{A}|.

This huge invariance group is both a source of difficulties and a big advantage in studying this Lagrangian.

Our starting point consists in considering the space of connection forms modulo this gauge group action. One of the main challenges in the field consists in proving the existence of a gauge
g
in which the connection form
A^{g}
is ‘‘optimally’’ controlled
3
3

3

The adjective “optimally” refers to the smallest possible classical function space that
A^{g}
belongs to.
by its Yang–Mills energy
\text{YM}(A^{g})=\text{YM}(A)
. For instance, in order to make the functional
YM
as much coercive as possible, a reasonable quest suggested by the abelian case (
G=U(1)
) in electromagnetism consists in looking for the
Coulomb condition
to be fulfilled. This amounts to finding a local gauge
g
such that

(I.5)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}d^{\ast}A^{g}=0&\mbox{ in }\mathbb{B}^{n}\\
\langle A^{g},x\rangle=0&\mbox{ on }\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}.\end{cases}

This condition is equivalent to the following non-linear elliptic PDE

(I.6)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}-\text{div}\left(g^{-1}\nabla g\right)=\mbox{div}(g^{-1}Ag)\ &\mbox{ in }\mathbb{B}^{n}\\
-g^{-1}\partial_{\nu}g=g^{-1}\left<A,x\right>g&\mbox{ on }\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}.\end{cases}

We shall come back to the difficulty of solving (
I.6
) later in this introduction but, assuming such a
g
has been obtained, we control

(I.7)

\displaystyle C^{-1}\|A^{g}\|^{2}_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}|dA^{g}|^{2}+|d^{\ast}A^{g}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{n}\leqslant\mbox{YM}(A)+\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|A^{g}\wedge A^{g}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{n}.

From the Sobolev embedding theorem, for
n>2
we have
4
4

4

For
n=2
, we have

W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\hookrightarrow\bigcap_{p=1}^{+\infty}L^{p}(\mathbb{B}^{n}).

(I.8)

\displaystyle W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\hookrightarrow L^{\frac{2n}{n-2}}(\mathbb{B}^{n}).

In dimension
n=3,4
, Hölder inequality implies that
L^{\frac{2n}{n-2}}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
. Hence, for
n\leqslant 4
we obtain the bound

(I.9)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}|A^{g}\wedge A^{g}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{n}\leqslant\|A^{g}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{4}\leqslant C\|A^{g}\|^{4}_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})},

and finally

(I.10)

\displaystyle\|A^{g}\|^{2}_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leqslant C\,\mbox{YM}(A)+\ C\|A^{g}\|^{4}_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}.

Assuming now that a smallness condition of the form
C\|A^{g}\|^{2}_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leqslant 2^{-1}
is known, one gets in return the “a priori” estimate

(I.11)

\displaystyle\|A^{g}\|^{2}_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leqslant C\,\mbox{YM}(A).

One of the main achievements of
[
35
]
is to convert the a priori estimate (
I.11
) into an existence result for (
I.6
) such that (
I.11
) eventually holds, provided
\mbox{YM}(A)
is small enough.

This achievement is not straightforward at all in dimension
n\geqslant 4
. A first attempt would be to use the variational nature of the problem. Indeed, equation (
I.6
) happens to be the Euler–Lagrange equation of

(I.12)

\displaystyle\|A^{g}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}|dg+Ag|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{n}.

Hence, independently of dimension, a solution to (
I.6
) can be obtained by a direct minimization of (
I.12
) and by applying the fundamental principles of the calculus of variations. Nevertheless, this variational strategy is hitting a serious regularity issue in the sense that, for a generic
A\in L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
, a minimizer
g
of (
I.12
) is a priori only in
W^{1,2}
and in general (
I.11
) is not satisfied by any of these minimizers
5
5

5

Even for a smooth data
A
, minimizers to the Dirichlet energy for maps from
\mathbb{B}^{4}
into
G
are known to be at most in
W^{2,(\frac{3}{2},\infty)}(\mathbb{B}^{4},G)
(see
[
27
]
) and are certainly not automatically in
W^{2,2}(\mathbb{B}^{4},G)
, which must be the case if
A^{g}
is in
W^{1,2}
.

Here, another dichotomy appears within the low dimensions
n\leqslant 4
. For
n<4
, because of the Sobolev embedding

(I.13)

\displaystyle W^{2,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\hookrightarrow C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{n}),

the group multiplication

(I.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{M}:W^{2,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n},G)^{2}&\rightarrow W^{2,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n},G)\\[5.69054pt]
(g,h)&\mapsto gh\end{split}

is smooth between the two Banach manifolds
W^{2,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n},G)^{2}
and
W^{2,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n},G)
. This allows to implement an argument based on the local inversion theorem in order to prove that any
A\in W^{1,2}(\wedge^{1}\mathbb{B}^{n}\otimes\mathfrak{g})
satisfying
\operatorname{YM}(A)<\varepsilon
admits a local gauge
g\in W^{2,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n},G)
solving (
I.6
) and (
I.11
).

Coming now to the critical dimension 4, the group multiplication map
\mathcal{M}
is still well-defined, since the group
G
is assumed to be compact, but
\mathcal{M}
ceases to be continuous. Moreover,
W^{2,2}(\mathbb{B}^{n},G)
looses its natural Banach manifold structure. These facts prevent implementing the strategy involving the direct use of the local inversion theorem. K. Uhlenbeck instead developed a very clever continuity argument leading to a
W^{1,2}
controlled representative satisfying (
I.11
), under small
\operatorname{YM}
-energy assumption.

To summarize, the balance between the linear part
dA
and the bilinear part
A\wedge A
of the curvature form can be settled in favor of the linear and more regularizing part
dA
as long as
\text{YM}(A)
is small enough and
up to dimension
4
. This enables to implement classical variational strategies for
YM
within the framework of
Sobolev connections
6
6

6

A Sobolev
W^{k,p}
-connection on a smooth principal
G
-bundle
\pi:P\rightarrow M^{4}
is given by a collection of
W^{k,p}

\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-forms
A_{U}
on each open set
U
over which
P
is trivial (i.e.
\pi^{-1}(U)\simeq U\times G
as principal
G
-bundle isomorphism) and related to each other by the classical gauge equivalence relations

(I.15)

\displaystyle A_{V}:=g^{-1}_{UV}dg_{UV}+g^{-1}_{UV}\,A_{U}\,g_{UV}\quad\mbox{ on }U\cap V

where
g_{UV}
are the
G
-valued transition functions defining the smooth bundle
P
(see for instance
[
11
]
).
on
smooth principal
G
-bundles
(see e.g.
[
28
]
for minimization procedures).

In dimension larger than
4
, the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,2}\hookrightarrow L^{4}
does not hold anymore. This fundamental fact compromises Uhlenbeck’s procedure to extract controlled gauges. Even worse, one can produce a sequence of smooth
\mathfrak{su}(2)
-valued 1 forms
\{A_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
on
\mathbb{B}^{5}
such that

(I.16)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}A_{k}\rightharpoonup A_{\infty}&\mbox{ weakly in }L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})\\
dA_{k}+A_{k}\wedge A_{k}\rightharpoonup F_{\infty}&\mbox{ weakly in }L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})\\
d^{\ast}A_{k}=0&\mbox{ in }\mathbb{B}^{5}.\end{cases}

and

(I.17)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Spt}\ d\left(\operatorname{tr}(F_{\infty}\wedge F_{\infty})\right)=\overline{\mathbb{B}^{5}}.

Assume that there exists
B\in W^{1,2}(\wedge^{1}\mathbb{B}^{5}\otimes\mathfrak{su}(2))
such that

(I.18)

\displaystyle F_{\infty}=dB+B\wedge B=F_{B}.

Then, for any smooth function
\varphi
compactly supported in
\mathbb{B}^{5}
the coarea formula gives

(I.19)

\displaystyle\int_{B^{5}}d\varphi\wedge\mbox{tr}(F_{B}\wedge F_{B})=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}d\mathcal{L}^{1}(s)\int_{\varphi^{-1}(s)}\mbox{tr}(F_{B}\wedge F_{B}).

Thanks to Sard’s theorem and Fubini’s theorem, for
\mathscr{H}^{1}
-a.e.
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
\varphi^{-1}(s)
is a smooth closed 4-dimensional manifold and the restriction of
B
to this submanifold is in
W^{1,2}
. On such a 4-dimensional manifold, by a straightforward strong approximation procedure for
B
in
W^{1,2}(\wedge^{1}\varphi^{-1}(s)\otimes\mathfrak{su}(2))
, we can derive the following identity from the classical expression of the
transgression form
for the second Chern class:

(I.20)

\displaystyle\mbox{tr}(F_{B}\wedge F_{B})=d\left[\mbox{tr}\left(B\wedge dB+\frac{1}{3}\,B\wedge[B,B]\right)\right].

This implies that

(I.21)

\displaystyle\int_{\varphi^{-1}(s)}\mbox{tr}(F_{B}\wedge F_{B})=0\quad\mbox{ for $\mathscr{H}^{1}$-a.e. }s\in\mathbb{R}.

Combining (
I.19
) and (
I.21
), we get

(I.22)

\displaystyle d\left(\mbox{tr}(F_{B}\wedge F_{B})\right)=0\quad\mbox{ in }{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}^{5}).

This fact is contradicting (
I.17
) and we conclude that the weak limit of the smooth curvatures
F_{A_{k}}
on
\mathbb{B}^{5}
cannot be, even locally, the curvature of a Sobolev
W^{1,2}
-connection. For sequences of smooth Yang--Mills fields (smooth critical points of YM) with uniformly bounded energy, the possibility for their weak limits
7
7

7

These objects are called
admissible Yang–Mills connections
in
[
34
]
.
not to satisfy (
I.22
) is not excluded at all. In fact, we believe that it is possible to produce such sequences where (
I.22
) is violated.

These facts left the variational geometric analysis community in some perplexity and the following question arose naturally:

What is the space of weak limits of curvatures of smooth Yang–Mills connections

in supercritical dimension?

The above considerations are excluding the space of the curvatures of Sobolev connections, which has been the unique framework adopted so far to approach the variational issues related to the Yang–Mills lagrangian in subcritical and critical dimension.

The work in
[
21
]
was motivated by this question and brought an answer to it in the abelian case. In this framework, a
2
-form
F
on a closed oriented connected surface
\Sigma
is the curvature of some complex line bundle
E
over
\Sigma
if and only if

(I.23)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}F\in 2\pi{\mathbb{Z}}\ \quad\mbox{ and then }\quad c_{1}(E)=\int_{\Sigma}F.

The authors introduced on
\mathbb{B}^{3}
the space of
weak
L^{p}
-curvatures

(I.24)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{p}_{\mathbb{Z}}:=\left\{F\in L^{p}(\mathbb{B}^{3})\ :\ \forall\,\varphi\in\operatorname{Lip}_{c}(\mathbb{B}^{3})\ \mbox{ and for $\mathscr{H}^{1}$-a.e. }s\in\mathbb{R},\ \int_{\varphi^{-1}(s)}F\in 2\pi{\mathbb{Z}}\right\}.

The main result in
[
21
]
establishes that for every
p>1
the space
\mathcal{F}^{p}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is sequentially closed for the weak convergence in
L^{p}
. This statement and some complementary results were made more precise and extended to higher dimensions in
[
4
]
and
[
5
]
.

Inspired by the abelian case, M. Petrache and the second author introduced the space of
weak connections
on a
n
-dimensional manifold
N^{n}
. The first main idea consists first, for the dimensions
n\leqslant 4
, in “wrapping together” the space of principal
G
-connections for any possible principal
G
-bundles in a single definition. We define

(I.25)

{\mathbb{A}}_{G}(N^{n}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\nabla:=(U_{i},A_{i})_{i\in I}\ ;\ (U_{i})_{i\in I}\mbox{ realizes an open cover of $N^{n}$}\\[8.53581pt]
A_{i}\in W^{1,2}(\wedge^{1}U_{i}\otimes\mathfrak{g}),\ \forall\ i\neq j\ \ \exists\,g_{ij}\in W^{2,2}(U_{i}\cap U_{j},G)\ \mbox{ s. t. }\\[8.53581pt]
A_{j}=A_{i}^{g_{ij}}:=g_{ij}^{-1}d{g_{ij}}+g_{ij}^{-1}\,A_{i}\,g_{ij}\quad\mbox{ on }U_{i}\cap U_{j}\\[8.53581pt]
\forall\ i\neq j\neq k\quad g_{ij}\,g_{jk}\,g_{ki}\equiv\operatorname{id}_{G}\quad\mbox{ on }U_{i}\cap U_{j}\cap U_{k}\\[5.69054pt]
|F_{\nabla}|\in L^{2}(N^{n})\end{array}\right\}.

This is the definition of Sobolev connections considered in the classical analytical works on gauge theory since the early eighties (
[
9
]
,
[
11
]
). In order to implement analysis arguments, we need to find some natural generalization of Sobolev connections to higher dimension that enjoys a good closure property. Aiming to do so, it is tempting to work with a single form to represent the connection. The problem of constructing a global gauge to any Sobolev connection in dimension at most 4 was considered for the first time in
[
19
]
.

Theorem I.1
.

[
19
]
Let
\nabla\in{\mathbb{A}}_{\operatorname{SU}(2)}(N^{n})
be a Sobolev connection of some principal
\operatorname{SU}(2)
-bundle over a closed oriented Riemannian manifold
N^{n}
of dimension
n\leqslant 4
. Then there exists
A\in L^{4,\infty}(\wedge^{1}N^{n}\otimes{\mathfrak{su}(2)})
such that, about every point, there exists locally a
W^{1,(4,\infty)}
trivialization in which

(I.26)

\displaystyle\nabla=d+A,

where
L^{4,\infty}
is the weak Marcinkiewicz space

(I.27)

\displaystyle L^{4,\infty}(N^{n}):\left\{f\mbox{ is measurbale and }\ \sup_{\lambda>0}\lambda\,\left|\left\{x\in N^{n}\ ;\ |f(x)|>\lambda\right\}\right|^{\frac{1}{4}}<+\infty\right\}\

with
|\cdot|
being the measure induced by the volume form of
N^{n}
.

Representing a smooth connection on a non trivial bundle by a global 1-form is obviously impossible if one does not give up regularity. In fact, for instance, if the bundle is a non trivial
\operatorname{SU}(2)
-bundle over the 4-sphere, one can prove that no smooth connection
\nabla
has a global representative in
L^{4}
. Hence, the regularity
L^{4,\infty}
is optimal in that sense
8
8

8

In
[
19
]
, the authors were asking the question whether a global gauge in the optimal space
L^{4,\infty}(\wedge^{1}N^{n}\otimes{\mathfrak{su}(2)})
and satisfying simultaneously the Coulomb condition
d^{\ast}A=0
. A partial answer to this question which is still open as such is given in
[
38
]
.
.

Theorem
I.1
is leading naturally to the following definition

(I.28)

\displaystyle A_{G}(N^{n}):=\left\{A\in L^{2}(\wedge^{1}N^{n}\otimes\mathfrak{g})\mbox{ : }F_{A}\in L^{2}(N^{n})\mbox{ and }\ \exists\mbox{ locally }g\in W^{1,2}\mbox{ s.t. }A^{g}\in W^{1,2}\right\}.

Thanks to this theorem, for
n\leqslant 4
we have

(I.29)

\displaystyle A_{\operatorname{SU}(2)}(N^{n})={\mathbb{A}}_{\operatorname{SU}(2)}(N^{n})

Finally, again for
n\leqslant 4
, one can consider the following apparently weaker definition

(I.30)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{G}(N^{n}):=\left\{A\in L^{2}(\wedge^{1}N^{n}\otimes\mathfrak{g})\mbox{ : }F_{A}\in L^{2}(N^{n})\mbox{ and }\exists\mbox{ locally }g\in W^{1,2}\mbox{ s.t. }A^{g}\in L^{4}\right\}.

This definition more flexible than the definition of
A_{G}
, because it extends to spaces
X^{n}
which are bi-Lipschitz homeomorphic to a smooth Riemannian oriented closed manifold
N^{n}
and
n\leqslant 4
. Moreover, we prove in the Appendix A of the present paper that for any Riemannian manifold of dimension less or equal than
4
and for any compact Lie group
G

(I.31)

\displaystyle A_{G}(N^{n})=\mathfrak{a}_{G}(N^{n}).

In
[
26
]
, a proof of the sequential weak closure of
\mathfrak{a}_{G}(M^{4})
under Yang–Mills energy control is given for every closed oriented 4-dimensional Riemannian manifold
N^{4}
. The proof is based on the analysis mostly developed by K. Uhlenbeck during the 80s in
[
35
]
,
[
36
]
and
[
37
]
, combined with some more recent arguments involving the use of interpolation spaces introduced in this context by the second author in
[
24
]
.

Coming now to the supercritical dimensions, as underlined earlier one would also wish to produce a class of “objects” containing smooth connections and which is weakly sequentially closed under Yang–Mills energy control exclusively. In order to do so, the main idea in
[
21
]
is to propose an inductive definition by mean of generic slicing. Let
n>4
and denote by
{\mathcal{L}}(N^{n})
the space of Lipschitz functions whose level sets are almost always bi-Lipschitz equivalent to a smooth manifold. We introduce the following space:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{G}(N^{n}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}A\in L^{2}(\wedge^{1}N^{n}\otimes\mathfrak{g})\mbox{ : }F_{A}\in L^{2}(N^{n})\\[8.53581pt]
\,\mbox{ s.t. }\,\forall\,\varphi\in{\mathcal{L}}(N^{n})\,\mbox{ and for $\mathscr{H}^{1}$-a.e. }s\in\mathbb{R}\\[8.53581pt]
\iota_{\varphi^{-1}(s)}^{\ast}A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\varphi^{-1}(s))\end{array}\right\},

where
\iota_{\varphi^{-1}(s)}
is the canonical inclusion of
\varphi^{-1}(s)
in
M^{m}
.

In
[
20
]
, a proof of the sequential weak closure of
\mathfrak{a}_{G}(M^{5})
under Yang--Mills energy control is proposed
9
9

9

The proof of the sequential weak closure in
[
20
]
is based on the Proposition 2.1 in the same paper. As discussed in Remark
II.1
,
[
20
, Proposition 2.1]
has the missing term
\|A\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
on the right-hand-side of the inequality (2.2). This was first noticed by S. Sil. Our Proposition
II.1
below is a suitable replacement of
[
20
, Proposition 2.1]
as explained in section II. We give a complete and detailed proof of the sequential weak closure of
\mathfrak{a}_{G}(M^{5})
under controlled Yang–Mills energy in
[
6
]
. More precisely, the authors show that

(I.35)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\,\,\forall\ \{A_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathfrak{a}_{G}(N^{5})\,\mbox{ s.t. }\,\displaystyle{\limsup_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\mbox{YM}(A_{k})<+\infty}\\[8.53581pt]
\,\,\exists\,\{A_{k_{n}}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}},\,\,A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(N^{5})\,\mbox{ and }\,\{g_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset W^{1,2}(N^{5},G)\\[8.53581pt]
\,\,\mbox{s.t. }A_{k_{n}}^{g_{n}}\rightharpoonup A\ \mbox{ in }L^{2}(N^{5})\,\mbox{ and }\,\mbox{YM}(A)\leqslant\displaystyle{\liminf_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\mbox{YM}(A_{k_{n}})}.\end{cases}

The proof of (
I.35
) uses a strong approximation property of elements in
\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
by connection forms which are smooth away from finitely many points, modulo gauge transformations. More specifically, in
[
20
]
the authors show that for any
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
there exists a sequence of
\{A_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
such that each
A_{k}
is gauge equivalent to a connection form in
\mathbb{B}^{5}
which is smooth away from finitely many points and

(I.36)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\,A_{k}\rightarrow A&\mbox{ strongly in }L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5}),\\[5.69054pt]
\,F_{A_{k}}\rightharpoonup F_{A}&\mbox{ weakly in }L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5}),\\[5.69054pt]
\,\mbox{tr}\left(F_{A_{k}}\wedge F_{A_{k}}\right)\rightharpoonup\mbox{tr}\left(F_{A}\wedge F_{A}\right)&\mbox{ weakly in }{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{B}^{5}).\end{cases}

The space
\mathcal{F}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
of smooth connections away from isolated points is the smallest space such that the strong approximation property (
I.36
) holds true. This makes
{\mathcal{F}}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
a natural subspace in
\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
, in the same way as the space
R^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{3},\mathbb{S}^{2})
of maps in the Sobolev space
W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{3},\mathbb{S}^{2})
that are smooth away from finitely many isolated topological singularities is the smallest subspace in
W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{3},\mathbb{S}^{2})
being sequentially dense with respect to the
W^{1,2}
-norm (see
[
3
, Theorem 4]
).

Because of the sequential weak closure property (
I.35
),
\mathfrak{a}_{G}(N^{5})
is a space in which variational problems related to the Yang–Mills lagrangian on
N^{5}
are well-posed. We can then define the notion of weak Yang–Mills connections.

Definition I.1

(Weak Yang–Mills connections)
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. We say that
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
is a
weak Yang–Mills connection
on
\mathbb{B}^{5}
if

(I.37)

\displaystyle d_{A}^{*}F_{A}=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\sum_{i=1}^{5}\partial_{x_{i}}(F_{A})_{ij}+[A_{i},(F_{A})_{ij}]\ \quad\forall\ j=1\cdots 5\quad\mbox{ distributionally on }\mathbb{B}^{5},

i.e.

(I.38)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}\left<F_{A},d_{A}\varphi\right>=0\qquad\forall\,\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\wedge^{1}\mathbb{B}^{5}\otimes\mathfrak{g}),

where we have

\displaystyle d_{A}\varphi:=d\varphi+[A\wedge\varphi]=d\varphi+A\wedge\varphi+\varphi\wedge A,\qquad\forall\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\wedge^{1}\mathbb{B}^{5}\otimes\mathfrak{g}).

and
\langle\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle
denotes the scalar product on
\wedge^{2}\mathbb{B}^{5}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}
.

Among weak Yang–Mills connections, we shall be particularly interested with the ones that satisfy the following
stationarity condition
(which is automatically satisfied by smooth solutions to (
I.37
) or by YM-energy minimizers for instance).

Definition I.2

(Stationary weak Yang–Mills connections)
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. We say that a weak Yang–Mills connection
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
on
\mathbb{B}^{5}
is stationary if

(I.39)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\operatorname{YM}(\Phi_{t}^{*}A)=0,

for every smooth
1
-parameter group of diffeomorphisms
\Phi_{t}
of
\mathbb{B}^{5}
with compact support.

If
A
is a stationary Yang–Mills connection, by standard methods it can be shown that the following
monotonicity property
holds true: for every given
x\in\mathbb{B}^{5}
, the function

(I.40)

\displaystyle\big(0,\dist(x,\partial\mathbb{B}^{5})\big)\ni\rho\to\frac{e^{c\Lambda\rho}}{\rho}\int_{B_{\rho}(x)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

is non-decreasing, where
c>0
is a universal constant and
\Lambda
depends on
B_{1}(x)
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in B_{\frac{1}{2}}(0),\\
0<\rho<\frac{1}{4}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{B_{\rho}(x)}|F_{A}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant C\ \int_{B_{1}^{5}(0)}|F_{A}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5},

for some constant
C>0
independent on
A
. We then naturally introduce the following spaces which are known as Morrey-Sobolev space for any domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}

(I.41)

\displaystyle M^{0}_{p,q}(\Omega):=\Bigg\{f\in L^{p}(\Omega)\mbox{ : }\lvert f\rvert_{M^{0}_{p,q}(\Omega)}^{p}:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\Omega,\\
\rho>0\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\rho^{n-pq}}\int_{B_{\rho}(x)\cap\Omega}|f|^{p}\ d{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\Bigg\}.

It is strongly motivated by the analysis of weak stationary Yang–Mills Fields to ask whether Uhlenbeck’s Coulomb gauge extraction extends in the higher dimension 5 to weak connections having a curvature with small Morrey
M^{0}_{2,2}
-norm. The following theorem answers positively to this question and is one of the main results of the present work.

Theorem I.2
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. There exists
\varepsilon_{G}>0
such that for every weak connection
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
satisfying

(I.42)

\displaystyle\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M^{0}_{2,2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}^{2}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{B}^{5},\\
\rho>0\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{B_{\rho}(x)\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}<\varepsilon_{G}

there exists
g\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{5},G)
such that

(I.43)

\displaystyle d^{\ast}A^{g}=0,

and

(I.44)

\displaystyle\rvert\nabla A^{g}\rvert_{M^{0}_{2,2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}^{2}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{B}^{5},\\
\rho>0\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{B_{\rho}(x)\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\sum_{i=1}^{5}|\partial_{x_{i}}A^{g}|^{2}\ d{\mathcal{L}}^{5}\leqslant C_{G}\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M^{0}_{2,2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}^{2},

where
C_{G}>0
is a constant depending only on
G
.

This result has been conjectured to hold in
[
20
]
. Such a Coulomb gauge extraction theorem has been first established in
[
34
]
for smooth connections and in
[
17
]
under the assumption that the connection can be approximated strongly by smooth connections with curvatures having small Morrey norm (
I.42
). Later on, the same statement was proved in a particular case assuming that the connection is a weak limit of smooth Yang–Mills fields (see
[
31
]
). Such weak limits are smooth away from a closed codimension 4 rectifiable set and and referred to as
admissible Yang–Mills connections
. We also remark that energy identities and bubbling analysis for Yang–Mills fields in supercritical dimension are due to the subsequent works of the second author and A. Naber–D. Valtorta, in
[
25
]
and
[
18
]
respectively.

In
[
31
]
, the authors prove a strong approximability property of admissible Yang–Mills connections by smooth connections with small Morrey norm (see
[
31
, Proposition 4.4]
). In
[
30
, Theorem 34]
, the author shows the existence of Morrey norm controlled local Coulomb gauges in supercritical dimension by exploiting an approximation procedure, in the same spirit as in
[
17
]
. However, approximating connections in the (stronger) Morrey norm requires additional assumptions
10
10

10

In particular, in
[
30
, Theorem 34]
the author exploits a “vanishing Morrey norm” condition.
which are not available in our context.

The main achievement of the present paper is to prove that
any weak connection satisfying (
I.42
)
(which includes all the previous cases) can be approximated by smooth connections with small Morrey norms.

Combining Theorem
I.2
and the main result in
[
17
]
we can derive the following
\varepsilon
-regularity statement by using the same arguments presented in
[
17
, Section 4]
.

Theorem I.3

(
\varepsilon
-regularity)
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. There exists
\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,1)
such that for every stationary weak Yang–Mills
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
satisfying

\displaystyle\operatorname{YM}(A)=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}<\varepsilon_{G}

there exist
g\in W^{1,2}(B_{\frac{1}{2}}(0),G)
such that
A^{g}\in C^{\infty}(B_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))
.

Finally, standard covering arguments give the following bound on the singular set of stationary weak Yang–Mills connections, which is the main result of the present paper.

Theorem I.4
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group and let
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
be a stationary weak Yang–Mills connection on
\mathbb{B}^{5}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{1}(\operatorname{Sing}(A))=0,

where
\mathscr{H}^{1}
is the
1
-dimensional Hausdorff measure in
\mathbb{R}^{5}
and
\operatorname{Sing}(A)\subset\mathbb{B}^{5}
is the singular set of
A
, given by
\operatorname{Sing}(A):=\mathbb{B}^{5}\smallsetminus\operatorname{Reg}(A)
where

\displaystyle\operatorname{Reg}(A):=\left\{x\in\mathbb{B}^{5}\mbox{ s.t. }\exists\,\rho>0,\ g\in W^{1,2}(B_{\rho}(x),G)\mbox{ s.t. }A^{g}\in C^{\infty}(B_{\rho}(x))\right\}.

Organization of the paper

As mentioned above, the proof of the main Theorem
I.2
consists in approximating strongly in
L^{2}
every weak connection
A
(i.e. every
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
) whose curvature has a small Morrey
M^{0}_{2,2}
-norm by a sequence of smooth
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-forms
A_{j}
having small Morrey
M^{0}_{2,2}
-norms.

Section II is devoted to the construction of the building blocks. It contains two main results, explaining how to extend in an “optimal way” inside a cube a given connection at its boundary. We propose two ways of extending this connection. First, we assume some smallness condition on the
L^{2}
-norms both of the curvature and of the connection at the boundary (Corollary
II.1
). Then, we assume smallness of the
L^{2}
-norm of the curvature only (Corollary
II.2
).

In Section III we introduce some terminology. We say that a cube is
good
if on its boundary both the
L^{2}
-norms of the curvature and of the connection are small. We call a cube
bad
if on its boundary just the
L^{2}
-norm of the curvature is small.
11
11

11

This terminology in the dichotomy between good and bad cubes is reminiscent of the one introduced in the framework of the strong approximation of weak connections in
\mathbb{B}^{5}
(
[
20
]
and
[
6
]
). Nevertheless, the meaning that we associate to it is different, in the sense that assuming the smallness condition of the Morrey norm allows us to decompose the domain into cubes at the boundary of which the
L^{2}
-norm of the curvature is always small. The dichotomy between good and bad cubes is made on the base of the smallness of
L^{2}
-norm of the connection only.
Then, in a second step, by the mean of the coarea formula and the mean value theorem we prove the existence of a so called
admissible covers
by small cubes of comparable sizes, so that the
L^{2}
-norm of the curvature is small on the boundary of each of the cubes.

Section IV is devoted to what is called the “first smoothification”. In the first smoothification, we replace the initial connection in every cube of a chosen admissible cover by mean of the extensions introduced in the previous section. When the cube is good, we use Corollary
II.1
whilst, if the cube is bad, we exploit by Corollary
II.2
. The main result in Section IV is Theorem
IV.1
. The consequence of the “first smoothification” is that the newly obtained connection forms
A_{i,\Lambda}
converging strongly in the
L^{2}
-norm to
A
as
i\rightarrow+\infty
and
\Lambda\rightarrow+\infty
, still having a small Morrey norm of the curvature, enjoys the following property: every trace on a generic cube of size comparable to the size of the admissible covering satisfies the small
L^{2}
-condition.

In Section V we shall proceed to the “second smoothification”, that is, the
L^{2}
strong approximation of the initial weak connection
A
by a sequence
A_{i}
of smooth connections whose curvature has small Morrey
M^{0}_{2,2}
-norm (Theorem
V.1
). The proof of the second smoothification goes as follows. Starting from the approximating sequences
A_{i,\Lambda}
given by the first smoothification, we take a grid of size comparable to the size of the admissible cover associated to
A_{i,\Lambda}
and we apply the replacement results of Section II (the building blocks Corollary
II.1
and Corollary
II.2
) on each of the disjoint cubes of the grid iteratively in such a way that, in the procedure, each cube to be replaced is facing at least one cube which has not been replaced yet.

In Section VI, we combine the approximation given by the second smoothification with the main result in
[
17
]
in order to prove our main theorem (Theorem
I.2
).

II.
The building blocks for the approximation theorems

II.1.
Extension of weak connections

In this subsection we build the fundamental statements that will be used in Section
IV
in order to prove the strong
L^{2}
-approximation theorems for weak connections (Theorem
IV.1
and Theorem
V.1
) under Morrey norm control. In particular, we will need Corollaries
II.1
and
II.2
to extend weak connections from the boundary of
5
-cubes to their interior. If we can assume the
L^{2}
-smallness of both the connection and its curvature on the boundary of the cube, then we will use Corollary
II.1
. In case we can only assume the
L^{2}
-smallness of the curvature of the connection on the boundary, we will exploit Corollary
II.2
.

II.1.1.
Extension under
L^{2}
-smallness of the connection and its curvature

To ease the reading, throughout this subsection we will denote by
``d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}"
and
``d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}"
the standard differential of
k
-forms respectively on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
and on the round sphere
\mathbb{S}^{4}
. More precisely we have

d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}:=\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{\ast}d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}},

where
\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}
is the canonical embedding of
\mathbb{S}^{4}
into
\mathbb{R}^{5}
. The following proposition is one of the building blocks of our approximation procedure.

Proposition II.1

(Harmonic extension under smallness condition on
F_{A}
and
A
)
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group and let
f:\mathbb{R}^{5}\to\mathbb{R}^{5}
be a bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism such that
f\in W_{loc}^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{5},\mathbb{R}^{5})
and

(II.1)

\displaystyle\langle d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i},d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{j}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}=0,\qquad\forall\,i,j=1,...,5\ \mbox{ s.t. }\ i\neq j.

Let

(II.2)

\displaystyle C_{f}:=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{5}\frac{1}{\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}}}.

There are constants
\varepsilon(G,f)\in(0,1)
and
C(G,f)
depending only on
G
,
C_{f}
and on
\|d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}
such that for any
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
satisfying

(II.3)

\displaystyle\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}<\varepsilon(G,f)

the following facts hold.

(i)

There exist
g\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
and a
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form
\tilde{A}\in L^{5}(\wedge^{1}\mathbb{B}^{5}\otimes\mathfrak{g})
such that

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\tilde{A}=A^{g}

and

\displaystyle\|A^{g}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(G,f)\ \left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right),

(II.4)

\displaystyle\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(G,f)\ \left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right),

(II.5)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|F_{\tilde{A}}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(G,f)\ \left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\left\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{3}\right),

for every constant
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\bar{A}
on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
.

(ii)

There exists
\tilde{g}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{5},G)
satisfying

(II.6)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{g}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|d\tilde{g}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C(G,f)\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right),

for every constant
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\bar{A}
on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
, such that the
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\hat{A}:=\tilde{A}^{\tilde{g}^{-1}}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
satisfies the following properties.

(a)

F_{\hat{A}}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
.

(b)

\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\hat{A}=A\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
.

(c)

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
be an open set such that
\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}
has a
4
-dimensional compact Lipschitz boundary which can be included in a union of
N
submanifolds of
\overline{\mathbb{B}^{5}}
of class
C^{2}
. Then we have

(II.7)

\|F_{\hat{A}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}))}\leqslant K_{G}\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\left\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{3}\right)

and

(II.8)

\|\tilde{A}\|_{L^{4}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant K_{G}\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\left\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right),

for every constant
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\bar{A}
on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
, where
K_{G}=K_{G}(\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})>0
depends only on
G
and on
\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}
(that is, on the number
N
of submanifolds containing
\partial(\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})
as well as their
C^{2}
norms), on
C_{f}
and on
\|d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}
.

Moreover,

(II.9)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\big\|\tilde{A}-f^{*}\bar{A}\big\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(G,f)\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right),

(II.10)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\big\|d\tilde{A}\big\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(G,f)\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right),

(II.11)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\big\|\hat{A}-f^{*}\bar{A}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(G,f)\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right),

for every constant
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\bar{A}
on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
.

Proof of Proposition
II.1
.

Notice that since
f=(f_{1},...,f_{5})
is a bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism, we have that
\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\neq 0
for every
i=1,...,5
. Let
\xi
be the constant
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
given by

(II.12)

\displaystyle\xi:=\sum_{i=1}^{5}\frac{1}{\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}}\langle A,d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}x_{i},

where
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1,...,5}
represent the standard euclidean coordinates on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
. We have

(II.13)

\displaystyle|\xi|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{5}\lvert\xi_{i}\rvert^{2}}\leqslant\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{5}\frac{1}{\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}}}\ \|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}=C_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

with

(II.14)

\displaystyle C_{f}:=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{5}\frac{1}{\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}}}.

Let
\eta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
be given by

(II.15)

\displaystyle\eta:=A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi=A-\sum_{i=1}^{5}\left\langle A,\frac{d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}}{\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\frac{d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}}{\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}}.

Notice that, since
\eta
is the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection of
A
on the linear subspace
\{d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\}_{i=1,...,5}^{\perp}\subset L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
, we have

(II.16)

\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

for every constant
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\bar{A}
on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}\eta=d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}A-d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}(\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi)=d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}(d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}\xi)=d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}A.

For future reference, we record the simple bound

(II.17)

\displaystyle\|f^{*}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{B}^{5}})}\leqslant C\|d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\,\lvert\xi\rvert\leqslant C\|d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\,C_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}=C\tilde{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

where
C_{f}>0
is given by (
II.14
) and we let
\tilde{C}_{f}:=\|d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\,C_{f}
.

Since
d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}(f^{*}\xi\wedge f^{*}\xi)=0
and
f^{*}\xi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
, for every exponent
p\in[1,+\infty)
there exists a unique
12
12

12

The form
\alpha
can be obtained variationally by minimizing

\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|\alpha|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}

among any form satisfying
d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}\alpha=f^{*}\xi\wedge f^{*}\xi
.

\alpha\in W^{1,p}(\wedge^{1}\mathbb{B}^{5}\otimes\mathfrak{g})
such that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}\alpha=f^{*}\xi\wedge f^{*}\xi\ &\mbox{ in }\mathbb{B}^{5},\\[0.0pt]
\displaystyle d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}^{*}\alpha=0&\mbox{ in }\mathbb{B}^{5},\\[0.0pt]
\displaystyle\alpha(\partial_{r})=0&\mbox{ in }\mathbb{B}^{5}.\end{cases}

Moreover, from
[
15
]
, for any
p\in[1,+\infty)
we have

(II.18)

\displaystyle\|\alpha\|_{W^{1,p}({\mathbb{B}^{5}})}\leqslant C_{p}\,\|f^{*}\xi\|^{2}_{L^{2p}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C_{p}\,\|f^{*}\xi\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C_{p}\tilde{C}^{2}_{f}\ \|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

where
C_{p}>0
depends only on
p\in[1,+\infty)
. In particular, we deduce

(II.19)

\|\alpha\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{B}^{5}})}\leqslant C\tilde{C}_{f}^{2}\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}.

We define

\displaystyle\omega:=\eta+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha=A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4}).

Observe that

(II.20)

\displaystyle\begin{split}F_{\omega}&=F_{\eta+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha}=d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}\eta+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(f^{*}\xi\wedge f^{*}\xi)+\eta\wedge\eta+\eta\wedge\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha\wedge+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha\wedge\eta+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(\alpha\wedge\alpha)\\[0.0pt]
&=\big(F_{\eta}+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(f^{*}\xi\wedge f^{*}\xi)\big)+\eta\wedge\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha\wedge+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha\wedge\eta+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(\alpha\wedge\alpha).\end{split}

Since
d(f^{*}\xi)=0
, we also have

(II.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}F_{A}&=F_{\eta+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi}=F_{\eta}+\eta\wedge\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi\wedge\eta+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(f^{*}\xi\wedge f^{*}\xi)\\[0.0pt]
&=\big(F_{\eta}+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(f^{*}\xi\wedge f^{*}\xi)\big)+\eta\wedge\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi\wedge\eta.\end{split}

Combining (
II.20
) and (
II.21
) we obtain

(II.22)

F_{\omega}=F_{A}-\eta\wedge\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi\wedge\eta+\eta\wedge\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha\wedge+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha\wedge\eta+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(\alpha\wedge\alpha).

Thus,

\displaystyle\|F_{\omega}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\lvert\xi\rvert\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\alpha\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\alpha\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\tilde{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\tilde{C}_{f}^{2}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\tilde{C}_{f}^{4}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{4}\right)

(II.23)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\tilde{C}_{f}(1+\tilde{C}_{f}\varepsilon_{G})\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\tilde{C}_{f}^{4}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\tilde{C}_{f}(1+\tilde{C}_{f}\varepsilon_{G}+\tilde{C}_{f}^{3}\varepsilon_{G}^{2})\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\varepsilon_{G}+\tilde{C}_{f}(1+\tilde{C}_{f}\varepsilon_{G}+\tilde{C}_{f}^{3}\varepsilon_{G})\varepsilon_{G}^{2}\right),

for some universal constant
C>0
. By assumption, since
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
and since the curvature of
A
is small enough in
L^{2}
-norm, there exists
\hat{g}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
such that
A^{\hat{g}}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
. We observe that

\displaystyle\omega^{\hat{g}}

\displaystyle=\hat{g}^{-1}d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}\hat{g}+\hat{g}^{-1}(\eta+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha)\hat{g}

\displaystyle=\hat{g}^{-1}d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}\hat{g}+\hat{g}^{-1}(A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi+\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha)\hat{g}=A^{\hat{g}}+\hat{g}^{-1}\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(\alpha-f^{*}\xi)\hat{g}\in L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4}).

and

\displaystyle\|F_{\omega^{\hat{g}}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

\displaystyle=\|F_{\omega}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C\left(\varepsilon_{G}+\tilde{C}_{f}(1+\tilde{C}_{f}\varepsilon_{G}+\tilde{C}_{f}^{3}\varepsilon_{G}^{2})\varepsilon_{G}^{2}\right).

If
\varepsilon_{G}
in (
II.3
) is small enough, we can apply Proposition
A.3
to
\omega^{\hat{g}}
and we get the existence of a gauge
h\in W^{1,4}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
such that, by letting
g:=\hat{g}h\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
, we have
d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\omega^{g}=0
and

(II.24)

\omega^{g}=A^{g}-g^{-1}\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\xi\,g+g^{-1}\,\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\alpha\,g.

Moreover,

(II.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\omega^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}&\leqslant C\|F_{\omega}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\tilde{C}_{f}(1+\tilde{C}_{f}\varepsilon_{G})\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\tilde{C}_{f}^{4}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{4}\right),\end{split}

where
C>0
only depends on
G
. We have

\displaystyle\|A^{g}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\omega^{g}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(G,f)\ \left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right).

Observe that by substituting
g
with
g\,g_{0}
for some
g_{0}\in G
we still have
d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\omega^{g}=0
and (
II.25
) with the constant
C>0
being unchanged. Since we have
d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g=g\omega^{g}-\omega g
, recalling that
\varepsilon_{G}<1
by assumption, we get

\displaystyle\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\ \left(\|\omega^{g}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\omega\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|F_{\omega}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\alpha\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right)

(II.26)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\tilde{C}_{f}(1+\tilde{C}_{f}\varepsilon_{G}+\tilde{C}_{f}^{3}\varepsilon_{G}^{2})\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+C\tilde{C}^{2}_{f}\ \|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\hat{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right),

where
\hat{C}_{f}:=\tilde{C}_{f}(1+2\tilde{C}_{f}+\tilde{C}_{f}^{3})
. Sobolev–Poincaré inequality gives the existence of
C>0
(independent on
A
) such that

(II.27)

\displaystyle\|g-\bar{g}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

where
\bar{g}
is the average of
g
on
\mathbb{S}^{4}
. Thus, we deduce the existence of
x_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{4}
such that

(II.28)

\displaystyle\lvert g(x_{0})-\bar{g}\rvert\leqslant C\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}.

Replacing
g
by
gg^{-1}(x_{0})
and combining (
II.27
) and (
II.28
) we obtain

(II.29)

\displaystyle\|g-\operatorname{id}_{G}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\hat{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right).

We denote
\tilde{g}:=g(x/|x|)\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
the radial extension of
g
in
\mathbb{B}^{5}
. A straightforward estimate gives

\displaystyle\|\tilde{g}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}\left|g\left(\frac{x}{|x|}\right)-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right|^{4}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}(x)\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

(II.30)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\int_{0}^{1}r^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{1}(r)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}\left|g-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right|^{4}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leqslant\|g-\operatorname{id}_{G}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\hat{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right).

Moreover, using (
II.26
), we get

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|d\tilde{g}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}\left\lvert d{g}\left(\frac{x}{|x|}\right)\right\rvert^{2}\frac{1}{\lvert x\rvert^{2}}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}(x)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(II.31)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\int_{0}^{1}r^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{1}(r)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}\lvert d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g^{2}\rvert\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\hat{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right).

Let
\tilde{\omega}
be the unique minimizers of

(II.32)

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}\big(\lvert d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}C\rvert^{2}+\lvert d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}^{*}C\rvert^{2}\big)\,dx^{5}\mbox{ : }\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}C=\omega^{g}\bigg\}.

Classical analysis for differential forms gives that
\tilde{\omega}
solves

(II.33)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}^{*}\tilde{\omega}=0&\mbox{ in }\mathbb{B}^{5},\\
d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}^{*}d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}\tilde{\omega}=0&\mbox{ in }\mathbb{B}^{5},\\
\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\tilde{\omega}=\omega^{g}=A^{g}+g^{-1}\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi)g&\mbox{ on }\partial\mathbb{B}^{5}\end{cases}

and that
\tilde{\omega}\in(W^{\frac{3}{2},2}\cap C^{\infty})(\mathbb{B}^{5})
. By classical elliptic regularity theory and Sobolev embedding theorem, we have the estimates

(II.34)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\tilde{\omega}\|_{W^{1,\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}&\leqslant C\|\tilde{\omega}\|_{W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\|\omega^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\\
&\leqslant C\big(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{4}\big),\end{split}

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
and on
f
(notice that the last inequality follows from (
II.25
)). Recall the continuous linear embedding

(II.35)

\displaystyle W^{1,\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})\hookrightarrow L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5}).

Hence, in particular

(II.36)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\tilde{\omega}\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}&\leqslant C\|\tilde{\omega}\|_{W^{1,\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\|\tilde{\omega}\|_{W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\|\omega^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{4}\right).\end{split}

We let
\tilde{A}\in L^{5}(\wedge^{1}\mathbb{B}^{5}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}})
be given by

(II.37)

\displaystyle\tilde{A}=\tilde{\eta}+f^{*}\xi=\tilde{\omega}-\alpha+\zeta+f^{*}\xi,

where we denote

(II.38)

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}:=\tilde{\omega}-\alpha+\zeta

and
\zeta
is constructed as follows. We apply Proposition
B.1
to
G
and we find a smooth Riemannian manifold
M_{G}
and a measurable map

(II.39)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}(g):\,\,

\displaystyle M_{G}\longrightarrow W^{1,\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5},G)

(II.40)

\displaystyle\quad p\longmapsto\,g_{p}

such that (
B.1
), (
B.4
) and (
B.6
) hold. We then choose

(II.41)

\displaystyle\zeta

\displaystyle:=\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi-\fint_{M_{G}}g_{p}^{-1}(\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi)g_{p}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)

(II.42)

\displaystyle\,=\fint_{M_{G}}\left(\operatorname{id}_{G}-g_{p}^{-1}\right)(\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi)g_{p}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)+\fint_{M_{G}}(\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi)\left(\operatorname{id}_{G}-g_{p}\right)\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p).

We notice that
\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\zeta=0
and
\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\tilde{\omega}=A^{g}+g^{-1}\,\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}(\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi)\,g
, so that
\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\tilde{A}=A^{g}
by construction
13
13

13

At this stage, in order to control the
L^{2}
-norm of
F_{\tilde{A}}
in
\mathbb{B}^{5}
it could be tempting to estimate separately
\|d\tilde{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}
and
\|\tilde{A}\wedge\tilde{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}
. This however is not going to lead to the expected estimate because each of the terms require to estimate respectively
\|d\alpha\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}
and
\|f^{\ast}\xi\wedge f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}
which would give in the r.h.s of (
II.5
) a term proportional to
\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}
as in
[
20
, Proposition 2.1]
but which is preventing to prove the desired approximability property
I.36
. While in the combination
d\tilde{A}+\tilde{A}\wedge\tilde{A}
these two contributions fortunately cancel each other which is one of the main advantage of this construction.
.

We have also

(II.43)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d\zeta&:=f^{\ast}\xi\wedge f^{\ast}\xi-\fint_{M_{G}}g_{p}^{-1}\,f^{\ast}\xi\wedge f^{\ast}\xi\,g_{p}\ dp-\fint_{M_{G}}dg_{p}^{-1}\wedge(\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi)\ g_{p}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\\[0.0pt]
&\quad+\fint_{M_{G}}g_{p}^{-1}\,(\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi)\wedge dg_{p}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\\[0.0pt]
&\,=\fint_{M_{G}}\left(\operatorname{id}_{G}-g_{p}^{-1}\right)\,f^{\ast}\xi\wedge f^{\ast}\xi\ g_{p}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)+\fint_{M_{G}}f^{\ast}\xi\wedge f^{\ast}\xi\ \left(\operatorname{id}_{G}-g_{p}\right)\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\\[0.0pt]
&\quad-\fint_{M_{G}}dg_{p}^{-1}\wedge(\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi)\ g_{p}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)+\fint_{M_{G}}g_{p}^{-1}\,(\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi)\wedge dg_{p}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p).\end{split}

Using (
B.3
), we have

(II.44)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|d\zeta\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}&\leqslant C\,\|f^{\ast}\xi\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\ \left\|\fint_{M_{G}}\left|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right|\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\\[0.0pt]
&\quad+C\,\|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\left\|\fint_{M_{G}}\left|d_{x}g_{p}\right|\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C_{f}\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\ \|dg\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant\,C\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\ \left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right)\end{split}

and, by Poincaré–Sobolev inequality,

(II.45)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\zeta\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}&\leqslant\|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{\infty}\ \left|\fint_{M_{G}}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}\left|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right|^{5}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right|^{\frac{1}{5}}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C_{f,G}\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\ \ \left|\fint_{M_{G}}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}\left|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right|^{5}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right|^{\frac{1}{5}}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C_{f,G}\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\ \ \left|\fint_{M_{G}}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}\left|d_{x}g_{p}\right|^{\frac{5}{2}}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right|^{\frac{2}{5}}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C_{f}\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\ \|dg\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\ \left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right).\end{split}

Hence,

(II.46)

\displaystyle\|d\zeta+\zeta\wedge\zeta\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\ \left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right).

We now explicitly compute

(II.47)

\displaystyle\begin{split}F_{\tilde{A}}&=d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}\tilde{A}+\tilde{A}\wedge\tilde{A}=d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}(\tilde{\omega}+f^{*}\xi-\alpha+\zeta)+(\tilde{\omega}+f^{*}\xi-\alpha+\zeta)\wedge(\tilde{\omega}+f^{*}\xi-\alpha+\zeta)\\[0.0pt]
&=F_{\tilde{\omega}}+F_{\zeta}-f^{*}\xi\wedge f^{*}\xi+\tilde{\omega}\wedge f^{*}\xi-\tilde{\omega}\wedge\alpha+\tilde{\omega}\wedge\zeta+{f}^{*}\xi\wedge\tilde{\omega}+{f}^{*}\xi\wedge{f}^{*}\xi-{f}^{*}\xi\wedge\alpha\\[0.0pt]
&\quad+{f}^{*}\xi\wedge\zeta+-\alpha\wedge\tilde{\omega}-\alpha\wedge{f}^{*}\xi+\alpha\wedge\alpha+\zeta\wedge\tilde{\omega}+\zeta\wedge{f}^{*}\xi-\zeta\wedge\alpha\\[0.0pt]
&=F_{\tilde{\omega}}+F_{\zeta}+(\tilde{\omega}\wedge{f}^{*}\xi+{f}^{*}\xi\wedge\tilde{\omega})-(\tilde{\omega}\wedge\alpha+\alpha\wedge\tilde{\omega})+(\tilde{\omega}\wedge\zeta+\zeta\wedge\tilde{\omega})\\[0.0pt]
&\quad-({f}^{*}\xi\wedge\alpha+\alpha\wedge{f}^{*}\xi)+({f}^{*}\xi\wedge\zeta+\zeta\wedge{f}^{*}\xi)-(\alpha\wedge\zeta+\zeta\wedge\alpha)+\alpha\wedge\alpha.\end{split}

Hence, we obtain

(II.48)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|F_{\tilde{A}}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}&\leqslant C\,\|d\tilde{\omega}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|\tilde{\omega}\|^{2}_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|F_{\zeta}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+C\,\|\alpha\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\,\|f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\\[0.0pt]
&\quad+\,C\,\|\zeta\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\,\left(\|f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|\alpha\|_{\infty}\right)+C\,\|\alpha\|_{\infty}^{2}\ \\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\,\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{3}\right).\end{split}

Observe that

(II.49)

\displaystyle\tilde{A}-f^{*}\bar{A}=\tilde{\eta}+f^{*}\xi-f^{*}\bar{A}=\tilde{\eta}+f^{*}(\xi-\bar{A}),

where
\tilde{\eta}:=\tilde{\omega}-\alpha+\zeta
. We have

(II.50)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}&\leqslant\|\tilde{\omega}\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|\alpha\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|\zeta\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{4}\right)+C\tilde{C}_{f}^{2}\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\\[0.0pt]
&\quad+C\tilde{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\hat{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right)\\[0.0pt]
&\quad+C\tilde{C}_{f}^{2}\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\hat{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right).\end{split}

We have also

(II.51)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|f^{*}(\xi-\bar{A})\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}&\leqslant C\|d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\|\xi-\bar{A}\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C\|d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\lvert\xi-\bar{A}\rvert=C\|d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{5}\lvert\xi_{i}-\bar{A}_{i}\rvert^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

where
C>0
is a universal constant. Notice that, by Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we have

(II.52)

\displaystyle\lvert\xi_{i}-\bar{A}_{i}\rvert^{2}=\frac{\lvert\langle A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A},d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\rvert^{2}}{\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{4}}\leqslant\frac{\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}}{\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}},\qquad\forall i=1,...,5,

which by (
II.51
) implies

(II.53)

\displaystyle\|f^{*}(\xi-\bar{A})\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\tilde{C}_{f}\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

for some universal constant
C>0
. We now need an estimate on
L^{2}
-norm of
\tilde{\eta}
. Using (
II.49
), (
II.16
), (
II.38
) and (
II.53
), we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{A}-f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|f^{*}(\xi-\bar{A})\|_{L^{5}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\left[\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{4}\right]+C\tilde{C}_{f}^{2}\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

(II.54)

\displaystyle\quad+C\tilde{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\hat{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\tilde{C}_{f}^{2}\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\hat{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\tilde{C}_{f}\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

where
C
only depends on
G
. Thus, we get (
II.9
) for
B:=\tilde{A}
. We have

(II.55)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|d\tilde{A}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}&\leqslant\|d\tilde{\omega}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|d\alpha\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|d\zeta\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{4}\right)+C\tilde{C}_{f}^{2}\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\\[0.0pt]
&\quad+C\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\ \left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right).\end{split}

Hence, we obtain (
II.10
).

We now prove (
II.11
). Notice that

(II.56)

\displaystyle\begin{split}F_{\hat{A}}&=d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}\hat{A}+\hat{A}\wedge\hat{A}=d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}(\tilde{A}^{\tilde{g}^{-1}})+\tilde{A}^{\tilde{g}^{-1}}\wedge\tilde{A}^{\tilde{g}^{-1}}\\[0.0pt]
&=\tilde{g}\,F_{\tilde{A}}\,\tilde{g}^{-1}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})\end{split}

and

(II.57)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\hat{A}&=\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(\tilde{g}d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}(\tilde{g}^{-1})+\tilde{g}\tilde{A}\tilde{g}^{-1})=\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(-d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}\tilde{g}\tilde{g}^{-1}+\tilde{g}\tilde{A}\tilde{g}^{-1})\\[0.0pt]
&=-d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}gg^{-1}+gA^{g}g^{-1}=-d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}gg^{-1}+g(g^{-1}d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g+g^{-1}Ag)g^{-1}\\[0.0pt]
&=A\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4}).\end{split}

We have

(II.58)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\hat{A}-f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}&\leqslant\|\hat{A}-\tilde{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|\tilde{A}-f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant\|\tilde{g}\,\tilde{A}\,\tilde{g}^{-1}-\tilde{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|\tilde{g}\,d\tilde{g}^{-1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|\tilde{A}-f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant\|\tilde{g}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\ \|\tilde{A}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|d\tilde{g}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|\tilde{A}-f^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}.\end{split}

Combining (
II.31
), (
II.54
), (
II.58
) and (
II.54
), we obtain (
II.11
).

Coming now to the trace estimates

(II.59)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\zeta\|_{L^{4}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant 2\ \|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\left\|\fint_{M_{G}}\left|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right|\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right\|_{L^{4}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant 2\ \|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\left\|\fint_{M_{G}}\left|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right|\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\fint_{M_{G}}\left|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right|\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C(G)\,\|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\left\|\fint_{M_{G}}\left|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right|\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C(G)\,\|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\bigg(\fint_{M_{G}}\left\|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C(G)\,\|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\left(\fint_{M_{G}}\left\|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C(G,\Omega)\,\|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\left\|g-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C(G,\Omega)\left(\tilde{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\tilde{C}_{f}^{2}\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right)\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\hat{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

We have also using (
II.43
)

\displaystyle\|d\zeta\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\|f^{\ast}\xi\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\left\|\fint_{M_{G}}\left|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right|\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}

\displaystyle\quad+C\,\|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\left\|\fint_{M_{G}}\left|dg_{p}\right|\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\|f^{\ast}\xi\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\fint_{M_{G}}\left\|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)

\displaystyle\quad+C\,\|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\fint_{M_{G}}\left\|dg_{p}\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\|f^{\ast}\xi\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\bigg(\fint_{M_{G}}\left\|g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(II.60)

\displaystyle\quad+C\,\|\alpha-f^{\ast}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\bigg(\fint_{p\in M_{G}}\left\|dg_{p}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Using Proposition
B.1
together with (
II.17
) and (
II.19
), we finally get

(II.61)

\displaystyle\|d\zeta\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C_{G}(\Omega)\big(\tilde{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\tilde{C}_{f}^{2}\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\big)\big(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\hat{C}_{f}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\big).\qquad\quad

Moreover, thanks to the continuous embedding
W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})\hookrightarrow W^{1,2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})
, we have

(II.62)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\tilde{\omega}\|_{L^{4}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}+\|d_{\partial\Omega}\tilde{\omega}\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}&\leqslant C(\Omega)\,\|\tilde{\omega}\|_{W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C(\Omega)\,\|\omega^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C(\Omega)\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{4}\right),\end{split}

where
C(\Omega)
depends only on the
C^{2}
-norm of the
C^{2}
components of
\partial(\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})
,
G
,
C_{f}
and
\|df\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}
. Combining now (
II.17
), (
II.19
), (
II.59
), (
II.61
), (
II.62
) and (
II.47
), we obtain (
II.7
).

Combining (
II.59
), (
II.62
), (
II.19
) and the definition of
\xi
, we obtain

(II.63)

\displaystyle\|\hat{A}^{\tilde{g}}\|_{L^{4}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}=\|\tilde{A}\|_{L^{4}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant K_{G}\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\left\|A-\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}f^{*}\bar{A}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right).

This concludes the proof of Proposition
II.1
.
∎

From now on, we will use the following notation:

\displaystyle\lvert x\rvert_{\infty}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{i=1,...,5}\lvert x_{i}\rvert,

\displaystyle\lvert x\rvert

\displaystyle:=\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{5}x_{i}^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},

for every
x=(x_{1},...,x_{5})\in\mathbb{R}^{5}
. We have obviously

\displaystyle\sqrt{5}^{-1}\,\lvert x\rvert\leqslant\lvert x\rvert_{\infty}\leqslant\lvert x\rvert\qquad\forall\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{5}.

Let
\varphi\in W_{loc}^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{5},\mathbb{R}^{5})
be the bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism given by

\displaystyle\varphi(x):=\begin{cases}\displaystyle{\frac{\lvert x\rvert}{\lvert x\rvert_{\infty}}}x&\mbox{ if }x\in\mathbb{R}^{5}\smallsetminus\{0\}\\
0&\mbox{ if }x=0.\end{cases}

Corollary II.1
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. Let
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
be any open cube with edge-length
k\varepsilon
, where
k>0
is a universal constant. There are constants
\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,1)
and
C_{G}>0
depending only on
G
such that for any
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\partial Q)
satisfying

(II.64)

\displaystyle\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}<\varepsilon_{G}

the following facts hold.

(i)

There exist
g\in W^{1,2}(\partial Q,G)
and a
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form
\tilde{A}\in L^{5}(Q)
such that for every 4-dimensional face
F
of
\partial Q
we have

(II.65)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\|A^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(F)}\hskip-5.69054pt&\leqslant C_{G}\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}\right),\\[0.0pt]
\iota_{F}^{*}\tilde{A}=A^{g}\end{cases}

and

(II.66)

\displaystyle\|F_{\tilde{A}}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{G}\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-2}\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}\,\|A-\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-3}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}^{3}\right),

(II.67)

\displaystyle\|dg\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{G}\left(\varepsilon\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\|A-\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}^{2}\right),

for every constant
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\bar{A}
on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
.

(ii)

There exists
\tilde{g}\in W^{1,2}(Q,G)
such that the
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form given by
\hat{A}:=\tilde{A}^{\tilde{g}^{-1}}\in L^{2}(Q)
satisfies the following properties.

(a)

F_{\hat{A}}\in L^{2}(Q)
.

(b)

\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\hat{A}=A\in L^{2}(\partial Q)
.

(c)

Let
Q^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
be an open cube such that
Q^{\prime}\cap Q
is a rectangle of minimum edge-length
\alpha\varepsilon>0
where
\alpha>0
is a universal constant. Then, we have

(II.68)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|F_{\hat{A}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q^{\prime}\cap Q)}^{2}&\leqslant C_{G}\,\big(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}^{2}+\varepsilon^{-4}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}^{2}\|A-\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}^{2}+\varepsilon^{-6}\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}^{6}\big),\end{split}

(II.69)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\tilde{A}\|_{L^{4}(\partial Q^{\prime}\cap Q)}\leqslant C_{G}\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-2}\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}\left\|A-\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}\bar{A}\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}\right),\end{split}

for every constant
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\bar{A}
on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
.

Moreover, we have the estimates

(II.70)

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\|\tilde{A}-\bar{A}\|_{L^{5}(Q)}\leqslant C_{G}\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\,\|A-\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-2}\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}^{2}\right),\end{array}

(II.71)

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\|d\tilde{A}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q)}\leqslant C_{G}\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\,\|A-\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-2}\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}^{2}\right),\end{array}

(II.72)

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\|\hat{A}-\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(Q)}\leqslant C_{G}\,\sqrt{\varepsilon}\,\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-1}\,\|A-\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\varepsilon^{-2}\,\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}^{2}\right),\end{array}

for every constant
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\bar{A}
on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
.

Proof of Corollary
II.1
.

Corollary
II.1
is deduced by applying Proposition
II.1
with
f=\varphi
to the 1-form
\big((\varepsilon\,\cdot\,+\,c_{Q})\circ\varphi\big)^{*}A
, where
c_{Q}
denotes the center of the cube
Q
, and then pulling the resulting data back on
Q
by
\big((\varepsilon\,\cdot\,+\,c_{Q})\circ\varphi\big)^{-1}
.
∎

II.1.2.
Extension under
L^{2}
-smallness of the curvature only

Proposition II.2

(Harmonic extension under smallness condition on
F_{A}
only)
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. There are constants
\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,1)
and
C_{G}>0
depending only on
G
such that for any
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
satisfying

(II.73)

\displaystyle\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}<\varepsilon_{G}

the following facts hold.

(i)

There exist
g\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
and a
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form
\tilde{A}\in(W^{\frac{3}{2},2}\cap C^{\infty})(\mathbb{B}^{5})
satisfying

(II.74)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\|\tilde{A}\|_{W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},\\[0.0pt]
\displaystyle\|A^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},\\[0.0pt]
\displaystyle\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\tilde{A}=A^{g},\end{array}\right.

and

(II.75)

\displaystyle\|dg\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C_{G}\big(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\big).

(ii)

There exists
\tilde{g}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{5},G)
such that the
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form
\hat{A}:=\tilde{A}^{\tilde{g}^{-1}}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
satisfies the following properties.

(1)

F_{\hat{A}}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
.

(2)

\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\hat{A}=A\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
.

(3)

(II.76)

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|\hat{A}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant C(G)\ \left(\|F_{A}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right).\end{array}

(4)

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
be an open set such that
\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}
has a
4
-dimensional compact Lipschitz boundary which can be included in a union of
N
submanifolds of
\overline{\mathbb{B}^{5}}
of class
C^{2}
. Then we have

(II.77)

\displaystyle\|F_{\hat{A}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant K_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

and

(II.78)

\|\tilde{A}\|_{L^{4}(\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant K_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

where
K_{G}=K_{G}(\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5})>0
depends only on
G
and on
\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}
(that is the number
N
of submanifolds containing
\partial\Omega
as well as their
C^{2}
norms).

Proof of Proposition
II.2
.

To ease the reading, throughout this proof we will denote by “
d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}
" and “
d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}
" the standard differential of
k
-forms on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
and on the round sphere
\mathbb{S}^{4}
respectively. First, notice that since
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
, by definition there exists locally
\hat{g}\in W^{1,2}
such that
A^{\hat{g}}\in W^{1,2}
. For
\varepsilon_{G}
in (
II.73
) small enough the existence of such a
g
is global and
A
has a representative globally on
\mathbb{S}^{4}
which is in
W^{1,2}(\wedge^{1}\mathbb{S}^{4})
. We can apply Uhlenbeck’s Coulomb gauge extraction theorem (see Proposition
A.1
) to
A^{\hat{g}}
and we get the existence of a gauge
h\in W^{1,4}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
such that, letting
g:=\hat{g}h\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
, we have
d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}A^{g}=0
and

(II.79)

\displaystyle\|A^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C(G)\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

where
C(G)>0
depends only on
G
. Observe that by changing
g
into
g\,g_{0}
we still have a solution of (
II.25
) with the constant
C(G)>0
being unchanged. We have
d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g=g\,A^{g}-A\,g
, hence

(II.80)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}&\leqslant C(G)\left(\|A^{g}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right)\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C(G)\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right).\end{split}

Poincaré inequality gives the existence of
C>0
(independent on
A
) such that

(II.81)

\displaystyle\|g-\bar{g}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C\ \|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

where
\bar{g}
is the average of
g
on
\mathbb{S}^{4}
. Thus, we deduce the existence of
x_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{4}
such that

(II.82)
