\displaystyle\lvert g(x_{0})-\bar{g}\rvert\leqslant C\ \|d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}.

Replacing
g
by
gg^{-1}(x_{0})
and combining (
II.81
) and (
II.82
) we obtain

(II.83)

\displaystyle\|g-\operatorname{id}_{G}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C\,\|dg\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C(G)\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right).

We denote
\tilde{g}:=g(x/|x|)
the radial extension of
g
in
\mathbb{B}^{5}
(here “
\lvert\,\cdot\,\rvert
” stands for the standard Euclidean norm of
x
). A straightforward estimate gives

(II.84)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{g}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant\|g-\operatorname{id}_{G}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C(G)\left(\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right).

Moreover, by the coarea formula we have

(II.85)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|d\tilde{g}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}&=\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{-2}\,\int_{\partial B_{r}(0)}\bigg\lvert dg\bigg(\frac{x}{\lvert x\rvert}\bigg)\bigg\rvert^{2}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}(x)\ d\mathcal{L}^{1}(\rho)\leqslant C\ \|dg\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C(G)\left(\|F_{A}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right).\end{split}

We now extend
A^{g}
by
\tilde{A}
that we choose to be the unique minimizer of

(II.86)

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}\big(\lvert d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}G\rvert^{2}+\lvert d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}^{*}G\rvert^{2}\big)\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\mbox{ : }\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}G=A^{g}\bigg\}.

Classical analysis for differential forms gives that
\tilde{A}\in(W^{\frac{3}{2},2}\cap C^{\infty})(\mathbb{B}^{5})
solves

(II.87)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}^{*}\tilde{A}=0,\\[0.0pt]
\displaystyle d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}^{*}d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}\tilde{A}=0,\\[0.0pt]
\displaystyle\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\tilde{A}=A^{g}.\end{array}\right.

Moreover, the following estimate holds:

(II.88)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{A}\|_{W^{1,\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\ \|\tilde{A}\|_{W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\ \|A^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C(G)\ \|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}.

Thus, (i) follows.
Let
\hat{A}:=\tilde{A}^{\tilde{g}^{-1}}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
. Notice that

(II.89)

F_{\hat{A}}=d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}\hat{A}+\hat{A}\wedge\hat{A}=d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}(\tilde{A}^{\tilde{g}^{-1}})+\tilde{A}^{\tilde{g}^{-1}}\wedge\tilde{A}^{\tilde{g}^{-1}}=\tilde{g}F_{\tilde{A}}\tilde{g}^{-1}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})

and

(II.90)

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\hat{A}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(\tilde{g}d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}(\tilde{g}^{-1})+\tilde{g}\tilde{A}\tilde{g}^{-1})=\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}(-d_{\mathbb{R}^{5}}\tilde{g}\tilde{g}^{-1}+\tilde{g}\tilde{A}\tilde{g}^{-1})

(II.91)

\displaystyle=-d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}gg^{-1}+gA^{g}g^{-1}=-d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}gg^{-1}+g(g^{-1}d_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g+g^{-1}Ag)g^{-1}=A\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4}).

We have also

(II.92)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|\hat{A}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}&=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|\tilde{g}\tilde{A}\tilde{g}^{-1}+\tilde{g}\,d\tilde{g}^{-1}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant 2\,\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|\tilde{A}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}+2\,\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|d\tilde{g}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C(G)\left(\|F_{A}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}+\|A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\right).\end{split}

Lastly, fix any open set
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
such that
\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}
has Lipschitz boundary which can be included in a union of
N
submanifolds of
\overline{\mathbb{B}^{5}}
of class
C^{2}
. Recalling that
\varepsilon_{G}<1
by assumption, we have

(II.93)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|F_{\hat{A}}\|_{L^{2}(X)}&=\|F_{\tilde{A}}\|_{L^{2}(X)}\leqslant C\left(\|\nabla\tilde{A}\|_{L^{2}\left(X)\right)}+\|\tilde{A}\|_{L^{2}\left(X\right)}^{2}\right)\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C(\Omega)\|\tilde{A}\|_{W^{\frac{3}{2},2}\left(\mathbb{B}^{5}\right)}\leqslant C(\Omega,G)\ \|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},\end{split}

where
C(\Omega,G)>0
depends only on
G
and on
\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}
. This concludes the proof of Proposition
II.2
.
∎

Completely analogously to the way we proved Corollary
II.1
, by exploiting Proposition
II.2
one derives from proposition
II.2
the following statement.

Corollary II.2
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. Let
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
be any open cube with edge-length
k\varepsilon
, where
k>0
is a universal constant. There are constants
\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,1)
and
C_{G}>0
depending only on
G
such that for any
A\in{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}(\partial Q)
satisfying

(II.94)

\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}<\varepsilon_{G}

the following facts hold.

(i)

There exist a gauge
g\in W^{1,2}(\partial Q,G)
and a 1-form
\tilde{A}\in W^{1,\frac{5}{2}}(Q)
such that for every 4-dimensional face
F
of
\partial Q
we have

(II.95)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\|d\tilde{A}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q)}+\|\tilde{A}\|_{L^{5}(Q)}\leqslant C_{G}\,\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)},\\
\|A^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(F)}\leqslant C_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)},\\
\iota_{F}^{*}\tilde{A}=A^{g}\end{cases}

and

(II.96)

\displaystyle\|F_{\tilde{A}}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{G}\,\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)},

(II.97)

\displaystyle\|dg\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{G}\big(\varepsilon\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}+\|A\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}\big).

(ii)

There exists
\tilde{g}\in W^{1,2}(Q,G)
such that the
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form
\hat{A}:=\tilde{A}^{\tilde{g}^{-1}}\in L^{2}(Q)
satisfies the following properties.

(a)

F_{\hat{A}}\in L^{2}(Q)
.

(b)

\iota_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}^{*}\hat{A}=A\in L^{2}(\partial Q)
.

(c)

Let
Q^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
be an open cube such that
Q^{\prime}\cap Q
is a rectangle of minimum edge-length
\alpha\varepsilon>0
where
\alpha>0
is a universal constant. Then, we have

(II.98)

\displaystyle\|F_{\hat{A}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q^{\prime}\cap Q)}\leqslant K_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)},

and

(II.99)

\|\tilde{A}\|_{L^{4}(\partial Q^{\prime}\cap Q)}\leqslant K_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)},

where
K_{G}>0
depends only on
G
.

Remark II.1
.

[
20
, Proposition 2.1]
, has a missing term on the right-hand-side of equation (2.2). This has been noticed by S. Sil. Proposition
II.1
is a suitable replacement of
[
20
, Proposition 2.1]
.

II.2.
Construction of optimally regular gauges on the boundary of cubes

We start by recalling the following optimal extension theorem, whose proof can be found in
[
22
, Theorem 2]
.

Proposition II.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
n\geqslant 1
and let
N\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{k}
be a closed embedded submanifold of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
. Then, for every
u\in W^{\frac{n}{n+1},n+1}(\mathbb{S}^{n},N)
there exists an extension
\tilde{u}\in W^{1,(n+1,\infty)}(\mathbb{B}^{n+1},N)
such that
\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}=u
in the sense of traces.

Next, we leverage on the Proposition
II.3
to prove the following gluing lemma.

Lemma II.1
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
n\geqslant 2
. Let

(II.100)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{S}_{+}^{n}&:=\{x=(x_{1},...,x_{n+1})\in\mathbb{S}^{n}\mbox{ : }x_{n+1}>0\}.\end{split}

Then, for every
g\in W^{1,n}(\mathbb{S}_{+}^{n},G)
there exists
\tilde{g}\in W^{1,(n+1,\infty)}(\mathbb{B}^{n+1},G)
such that
\tilde{g}|_{\mathbb{S}_{+}^{n}}=g
.

Proof of Lemma
II.1
.

Let

(II.101)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{-}^{n}

\displaystyle:=\{x=(x_{1},...,x_{n+1})\in\mathbb{S}^{n}\mbox{ : }x_{n+1}<0\}.

and define
h\in W^{1,n}(\mathbb{S}_{-}^{n},G)
by

(II.102)

\displaystyle h(x_{1},...,x_{n+1}):=g(x_{1},...,-x_{n+1})\qquad\forall\,x=(x_{1},...,x_{n+1})\in\mathbb{S}_{-}^{n}.

Let
\hat{g}\in W^{1,n}(\mathbb{S}^{n},G)
be given by

(II.103)

\displaystyle\hat{g}:=\begin{cases}g&\mbox{ on }\mathbb{S}_{+}^{n}\\
h&\mbox{ on }\mathbb{S}_{-}^{n}\end{cases}

Since
W^{1,n}(\mathbb{S}^{n},G)\hookrightarrow W^{\frac{n}{n+1},n+1}(\mathbb{S}^{n},G)
, by Proposition
II.3
there exists
\tilde{g}\in W^{1,(n+1,\infty)}(\mathbb{B}^{n+1},N)
such that
\tilde{g}|_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}=\hat{g}
in the sense of traces. Since
\hat{g}=g
on
\mathbb{S}_{+}^{n}
, this concludes the proof of Lemma
II.1
.
∎

Corollary II.3
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. There exist constants
\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,1)
and
C_{G}>0
depending only in
G
such that the following holds. Let
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
be an open cube in
\mathbb{R}^{5}
and let
\mathscr{F}
be any non-empty proper subset of
4
-dimensional faces of
\partial Q
. Let

(II.104)

\displaystyle\Omega:=\bigcup_{F\in\mathscr{F}}F.

Then, for every
g\in W^{1,4}(\Omega,G)
there exists an extension
\tilde{g}\in W^{1,5}(Q,G)
such that
\tilde{g}|_{\Omega}=g
in the sense of traces.

Proof of Corollary
II.3
.

Let
\Phi:\overline{Q}\to\overline{\mathbb{B}^{5}}
be a bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism such that

(II.105)

\displaystyle\Omega=\mathbb{S}_{+}^{4},\qquad\partial\Omega=\Gamma,\qquad\partial Q\smallsetminus\Omega=\mathbb{S}_{-}^{4}.

By apply Lemma
II.1
to
(\Phi^{-1})^{*}g\in W^{1,4}(\mathbb{S}_{+}^{4},G)
and pullback the resulting extension on
\mathbb{B}^{5}
by
\Phi
we get the desired
\tilde{g}
. This concludes the proof of Corollary
II.3
.
∎

III.
The notion of admissible covers and good and bad cubes

III.1.
The notion of admissible cubic
\varepsilon_{i}
-covers

From now on, for every
c\in\mathbb{R}^{5}
and
\varepsilon>0
we let

\displaystyle Q_{\varepsilon}(c):=\bigg(\hskip-4.2679pt-\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\bigg)^{5}+c

be the open cube with center
c
, edge-length
\varepsilon>0
and faces parallel to the coordinate planes. We shall be using the following definition.

Definition III.1
.

Let
A\in{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
,
c\in\mathbb{R}^{5}
and
\rho>0
. We say that
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{G}(Q_{\rho}(c))
if there exists an
L^{2}
1-form that we denote

\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}^{\ast}A
such that

(i)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\rho-\varepsilon}^{\rho+\varepsilon}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\left|\iota_{D_{r}}^{\ast}\iota_{\partial Q_{r}(c)}^{\ast}A-\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}^{\ast}A\right|^{2}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{1}(r)=0,

where
D_{r}(x):=\frac{\rho}{r}\,(x-c)+c
.

(ii)

\displaystyle\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}^{\ast}A\in{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}(\partial Q_{\rho}(c)).

As a direct consequence of Fubini and Lebesgue theorems, we have the following proposition.

Proposition III.1
.

Let
A\in{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}(\mathbb{B}^{5})
and
c\in\mathbb{B}^{5}
, then for
\mathcal{L}^{1}
-a.e.
\rho>0
such that
Q_{\rho}(c)\subset{\mathbb{B}}^{5}
there holds
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{G}(\partial Q_{\rho}(c))
. A positive number
\rho
such that
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{G}(\partial Q_{\rho}(c))
is called an admissible edge-length for
A
.

For the purposes of the present subsection, given any
\varepsilon\in\big(0,\frac{1}{4}\big)
we let

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon\mathbb{Z}^{5}\cap\overline{Q_{1-2\varepsilon}(0)}.

Note that

(III.1)

\displaystyle\#\{c^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}\mbox{ : }Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)\cap Q_{2\varepsilon}(c^{\prime})\neq\emptyset\}\leqslant N\qquad\forall\,c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon},

where
N\in\mathbb{N}
is independent on
\varepsilon
.

Lemma III.1

(Choice of an admissible cubic cover)
.

Let
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. There exists a universal constant
K>0
such that for a sequence
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\in\big(0,\frac{1}{4}\big)
satisfying
\varepsilon_{i}\to 0
for
i\to+\infty
, for
i
large enough we can find a family of admissible edge-lengths
\{\rho_{i,c}\}_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}}\subset\big(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon_{i},2\varepsilon_{i}\big)
for which the following facts hold.

(i)

A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{G}(\partial Q_{\rho_{i,c}}(c))
for every
c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
.

(ii)

For every
c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
we have

(III.2)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{\rho_{i,c}}(c)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{K}{2\varepsilon_{i}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

and

(III.3)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{\rho_{i,c}}(c)}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{K}{2\varepsilon_{i}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

(iii)

It holds that

(III.4)

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho_{i,c}}(c)}\big\lvert A-(A)_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}=0,

(III.5)

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}}\int_{Q_{\rho_{i,c}}(c)}\big\lvert A-(A)_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}=0

and

(III.6)

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho_{i,c}}(c)}\big\lvert F_{A}-(F_{A})_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}=0,

where we have used the following notation:

(A)_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}:=\fint_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}A\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\quad\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\quad(F_{A})_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}:=\fint_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}F_{A}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

Proof of Lemma
III.1
.

Fix any
\varepsilon\in\big(0,\frac{1}{4}\big)
. By assumption, for every
c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}
there exists a full
\mathcal{L}^{1}
-measure set
R_{A,c}\subset\big(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon,2\varepsilon\big)
such that
\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}^{*}A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\partial Q_{\rho}(c))
for every
\rho\in R_{A,c}
. Given a constant
K>0
(to be fixed), for any
c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}
we define the set

(III.7)

\displaystyle E_{\varepsilon,K,c}:=\bigg\{\rho\in\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon,2\varepsilon\bigg)\mbox{ s.t. }A\notin{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}(\partial Q_{\rho}(c))\quad\mbox{or }\quad\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}>\frac{K}{2\varepsilon}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg\}.

By integration on
E_{\varepsilon,K,c}
and Fubini together with mean value theorem we get

(III.8)

\displaystyle\frac{K}{2\varepsilon}\bigg(\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg)\mathcal{L}^{1}(E_{\varepsilon,K,c})

\displaystyle<\int_{E_{\varepsilon,K,c}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{1}(\rho)

(III.9)

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{0}^{2\varepsilon}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{1}(\rho)

(III.10)

\displaystyle=\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

This implies that

(III.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{1}(E_{\varepsilon,K,c})<\frac{2\varepsilon}{K},\qquad\forall\,c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}.

Fix
i\in\mathbb{N}
and let
A_{i}\in C_{c}^{\infty}({{\wedge}}^{1}Q_{1}^{5}(0)\otimes\mathfrak{g})
be such that

(III.12)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert A_{i}-A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant 2^{-i}.

Let

(III.13)

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon,K,i}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{\rho\in\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon,2\varepsilon\bigg)\mbox{ s.t. }\varepsilon\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\lvert A_{i}-A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}>K\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert A_{i}-A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg\},

(III.14)

\displaystyle H_{\varepsilon,K,i}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{\rho\in\bigg(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon,2\varepsilon\bigg)\mbox{ s.t. }f_{i}(\rho)>K\fint_{\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon}^{2\varepsilon}f_{i}(\rho)\,d\mathcal{L}^{1}(\rho)\bigg\},

with

(III.15)

\displaystyle f_{i}(\rho):=\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\big\lvert A_{i}-(A_{i})_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}.

Notice that, again by Fubini theorem together with mean value theorem and integration as above, we get

(III.16)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{L}^{1}(G_{\varepsilon,K,i})&\leqslant\frac{C\varepsilon}{K}\\[0.0pt]
\mathcal{L}^{1}(H_{\varepsilon,K,i})&\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{K}\end{split}

for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
, where
C>0
is a universal cover. By Fubini theorem, the mean value theorem and Poincaré inequality, we have

(III.17)

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon}^{2\varepsilon}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\big\lvert A_{i}-(A_{i})_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{1}(\rho)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\big\lvert A_{i}-(A_{i})_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

(III.18)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}C_{P}\big(Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)\big)\int_{Q_{\frac{3\varepsilon}{4}}(c)}\lvert\nabla A_{i}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

(III.19)

\displaystyle\leqslant 4C_{P}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\lvert\nabla A_{i}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

(III.20)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert\nabla A_{i}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

where we denote
C_{P}(\Omega)>0
for the open and bounded domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
and
C>0
is universal. Hence for any
\rho\in(G_{\varepsilon,K,i}\cup H_{\varepsilon,K,i})^{c}
(where the superscript “
c
” denotes the complement of the set in
(\varepsilon,2\varepsilon)
) one deduces

(III.21)

\displaystyle\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\big\lvert A_{i}-(A_{i})_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant K\fint_{\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon}^{2\varepsilon}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\lvert A_{i}-(A_{i})_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{1}(\rho)

(III.22)

\displaystyle\leqslant KC\varepsilon\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert\nabla A_{i}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

Moreover, for every
\rho\in(\varepsilon,2\varepsilon)
we have

(III.23)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\big\lvert(A_{i})_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}-(A)_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}|\partial Q_{\rho}(c)|\,\left(\fint_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}|A-A_{i}|\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}\right)^{2}

(III.24)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}|A-A_{i}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant C\ 2^{-i}.

Hence, by triangle inequality, we get that for every
\rho\in(G_{\varepsilon,K,i}\cup H_{\varepsilon,K,i})^{c}
we have

(III.25)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\big\lvert A-(A)_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\big(K+C\big)2^{-i}+KC\varepsilon\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert\nabla\tilde{A}_{i}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

Analogously, we consider
F_{i}\in C_{c}^{\infty}({{\wedge}}^{2}Q_{1}^{5}(0)\otimes\mathfrak{g})
such that

(III.26)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{i}-F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,dx^{5}\leqslant 2^{-i}

and we get that for every
\varepsilon\in\big(0,\frac{1}{4}\big)
and for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
there are two
\mathcal{L}^{1}
-measurable sets
G_{\varepsilon,K,i}^{\prime},H_{\varepsilon,K,i}^{\prime}\subset\big(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon,2\varepsilon\big)
satisfying

(III.27)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{L}^{1}(G_{\varepsilon,K,i}^{\prime})&\leqslant\frac{C\varepsilon}{K}\\[0.0pt]
\mathcal{L}^{1}(H_{\varepsilon,K,i}^{\prime})&\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{K}\end{split}

such that for every
\rho\in(G_{\varepsilon,K,i}^{\prime}\cup H_{\varepsilon,K,i}^{\prime})^{c}
we have

(III.28)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\big\lvert F_{A}-(F_{A})_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\big(K+C\big)2^{-i}+KC\varepsilon\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert\nabla F_{i}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

Now, first we let
\tilde{K}>0
be big enough (independent on
\varepsilon
and
i
) so that

(III.29)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{1}(G_{\varepsilon,\tilde{K},i}\cup H_{\varepsilon,\tilde{K},i}\cup G_{\varepsilon,\tilde{K},i}^{\prime}\cup H_{\varepsilon,\tilde{K},i}^{\prime})\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

Then, we notice that for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
\varepsilon_{i}<2^{-(i+1)}
small enough so that

(III.30)

\displaystyle\tilde{K}C\varepsilon_{i}\bigg(\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert\nabla A_{i}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert\nabla F_{i}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg)\leqslant 2^{-(i+1)}.

Then, for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
and for every
c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
we pick
\rho_{i,c}>0
in the non-empty set

(III.31)

\displaystyle(G_{\varepsilon_{i},\tilde{K},i}\cup H_{\varepsilon_{i},\tilde{K},i}\cup G_{\varepsilon_{i},\tilde{K},i}^{\prime}\cup H_{\varepsilon_{i},\tilde{K},i}^{\prime})^{c}\cap R_{A,c}\cap E_{\varepsilon_{i},\tilde{K},c}.

Notice that, for such admissible edge-lengths, we have

(III.32)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho_{i,c}}(c)}\big\lvert A-(A)_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\big(K+C\big)\,2^{-i}+2^{-(i+1)}

and

(III.33)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho_{i,c}}(c)}\big\lvert F_{A}-(F_{A})_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}\big\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\big(K+C\big)\,2^{-i}+2^{-(i+1)}.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
III.1
.
∎

Definition III.2
.

Let
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. Under the same notation that we have used in the previous Lemma
III.1
, for every
\varepsilon_{i}\in\big(0,\frac{1}{4}\big)
we say that
\varepsilon_{i}
is an
admissible scale
for
A
and that the collection of cubes
\{Q_{\rho_{i,c}}(c)\}_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}}
is a
admissible cubic
\varepsilon_{i}
-cover
relative to
A
.

Remark III.1
.

Let
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. Let
\varepsilon\in\big(0,\frac{1}{4}\big)
and let
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}
be an admissible cubic
\varepsilon
-cover relative to
A
. For every
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}
, denote by
\bar{A}_{Q}
the constant
2
-form given by
(A)_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c_{Q})}
, where
c_{Q}
denotes the center of
Q
. Notice that, by Jensen inequality, we have

(III.34)

\displaystyle\lvert\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\leqslant\fint_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},\qquad\forall\,Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}.

We have

(III.35)

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}}\bigg(\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}+\int_{\partial Q}\lvert\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)

(III.36)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}}\bigg(\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}+\varepsilon^{4}\lvert\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\bigg)

(III.37)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}}\bigg(\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}+\varepsilon^{4}\fint_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg)

(III.38)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bigg(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg),

where
C>0
is independent on
\varepsilon
. By (
III.4
), for
\varepsilon
small enough we have

(III.39)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

This implies that for such small values of the parameter
\varepsilon
it holds that

(III.40)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant C\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

where
C>0
is independent on
\varepsilon
. Exactly by the same procedure, we get that

(III.41)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant C\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

5
for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small.

III.2.
The notion of good and bad cubes

Definition III.3

(Good and bad cubes)
.

Let
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. Let
\varepsilon\in\big(0,\frac{1}{4}\big)
be an admissible scale for
A
and let
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}
be an admissible cubic
\varepsilon
-cover relative to
A
.
Given any
\Lambda>0
, we say that
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}
is a
\Lambda
-
good
cube if the following conditions hold:

(1)

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{3}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}}
,

(2)

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{3}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}}
,

(3)

\displaystyle{\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}}
,

(4)

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\Lambda^{-1}\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}}
,

(5)

\displaystyle{\fint_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant\Lambda}
.

Otherwise, we say that
Q
is a
\Lambda
-
bad
cube. We denote by
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,\Lambda}^{g}
the set of all the
\Lambda
-good cubes and
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,\Lambda}^{b}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}\smallsetminus\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon,\Lambda}^{g}
.

The following technical lemma is important for our argument. Given a sequence of admissible scales
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
, the lemma states that as
i\in\mathbb{N}
increases, asymptotically, the
L^{2}
-norm of
A
on the
\Lambda
-bad cubes is controlled by the
L^{2}
norm of
A
on the union of cubes where property (5) in Definition
III.3
fails.

Lemma III.2
.

Let
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
and let
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\big(0,\frac{1}{4}\big)
be a sequence of admissible scales for
A
. Let
\Lambda>0
and let
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
be an admissible cubic
\varepsilon_{i}
-cover relative to
A
and
\Lambda
, for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, for some universal constant
C>0
(depending on the intersection property of the cubic cover) there holds

(III.42)

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant C\lim_{i\to+\infty}\int_{\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A}}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

where
\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
is given by

(III.43)

\displaystyle\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A}:=\bigcup\bigg\{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}\mbox{ s.t. }\fint_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}>\Lambda\bigg\}.

Proof of Lemma
III.2
.

Observe the following.

•

If
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
is such that (1) fails, we have

(III.44)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}>\varepsilon_{i}^{\frac{9}{2}}\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

•

If
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
is such that (2) fails, we have

(III.45)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}>\varepsilon_{i}^{\frac{9}{2}}\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

•

If
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
is such that (3) fails, we have

(III.46)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}>\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

•

If
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
is such that (4) fails, we have

(III.47)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}>\varepsilon_{i}^{5}\Lambda^{-1}\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

Summing up over the appropriate cubes and by Remark
III.1
, for
\varepsilon_{i}>0
small enough we get the following set of estimates:

(III.48)

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}\mbox{ s.t. (1) fails}\})<C\varepsilon_{i}^{-\frac{9}{2}},

(III.49)

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}\mbox{ s.t. (2) fails}\})<C\bigg(\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg)\bigg(\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg)^{-1}\varepsilon_{i}^{-\frac{9}{2}},

(III.50)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}\\
\text{ s.t. (3) fails}\end{subarray}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}<\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4},

(III.51)

\displaystyle\operatorname{card}(\{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}\mbox{ s.t. (4) fails}\})<\Lambda\bigg(\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg)^{-1}\varepsilon_{i}^{-5}\bigg(\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg).

In particular, we note that the
\mathcal{L}^{5}
-measure of the union of the cubes in
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
such that (1),(2),(4) fail vanishes at the limit
i\to+\infty
. Thus,

(III.52)

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}\mbox{ s.t.}\\
(1),(2),(3),(4)\\
\mbox{ fail}\end{subarray}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}=0.

Finally, we get

(III.53)

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant\lim_{i\to+\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}\\
\text{ s.t. (5) fails}\end{subarray}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

and the statement follows by the intersection properties of the cubic cover. This concludes the proof of Lemma
III.2
.
∎

Remark III.2
.

Let
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. Let
\varepsilon\in\big(0,\frac{1}{4}\big)
be a good scale for
A
and let
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}
be an admissible cubic
\varepsilon
-cover relative to
A
. Notice that the property (3) in Definition
III.3
immediately implies that

(III.54)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A-\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}+\int_{\partial Q}\lvert\bar{A}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

(III.55)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+C\varepsilon^{4}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{5}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

for every good
\Lambda
-good cube
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}
, where
C>0
depends only on the dimension.

IV.
The first smoothification

The goal of this section is to approximate in
L^{2}
-norm any weak connection
A
whose curvature is small in Morrey norm by forms
A_{i,\Lambda}
whose curvature is suitably controlled in
L^{2}
-norm on a set of good slices at the same scale
\rho
.

Lemma IV.1
.

Let
Q=Q_{\rho}(c)\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
be an open cube in
\,\mathbb{R}^{5}
. Fix any
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
A\in L^{2}(\partial Q)
with
F_{A}\in L^{2}(\partial Q)
. Let
\{Q_{i}\}_{i=0,...,k-1}
be a collection of open cubes such that
\partial Q
is transversal to
\partial Q_{i}
for all
i
and assume that
\partial Q\subset\bigcup_{i=0}^{k-1}Q_{i}
,
A\in{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}(\partial Q\cap Q_{i}))
for every
i=0,...,k-1
. Then, there exists
\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,1)
depending only on
G
such that if

(IV.1)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}<\varepsilon_{G}

we can find a global gauge
g\in W^{1,2}(\partial Q,G)
such that
A^{g}\in L^{4}(\partial Q)
.

Proof of Lemma
IV.1
.

Since
A\in{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}(\partial Q\cap Q_{i}))
and since
\partial Q\cap Q_{i}
is bi-Lipschitz homeomorphic to the
4
-dimensional ball, there exists
h_{i}\in W^{1,2}(\partial Q\cap Q_{i},G)
such that
A^{h_{i}}\in L^{4}(\partial Q\cap Q_{i})
. We consider
\psi_{c,\rho}:=(\rho\,\cdot\,+\,c)\circ\varphi
which realizes a bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism
from
\mathbb{S}^{4}
into
\partial Q
and we denote
U_{i}
the Lipschitz open subset of
\mathbb{S}^{4}
given by
U_{i}:=\psi_{c,\rho}^{-1}(\partial Q\cap Q_{i})
. Let
(\omega_{i})_{i=0\cdots k-1}
be an open cover of
\mathbb{S}^{4}
such that
\overline{\omega_{i}}\subset U_{i}
. We then cover each

\omega_{i}
by finitely many smooth convex geodesic balls
(V_{ij})_{j\in I_{i}}
of
S^{4}
with
V_{il}\subset U_{i}
so that the whole cover given by the
V_{ij}
is a good cover of
\mathbb{S}^{4}
(i.e. the intersections of the
V_{ij}
s are again diffeomorphic to balls) by convex geodesic balls. To simplify the notations we re-index the cover

(V_{ij})_{i\in\{0\cdots k-1\},j\in I_{i}}
by
(O_{l})_{l=1\cdots L}
and we denote by
i_{l}
the index
i\in\{0\cdots k-1\}
such that
O_{l}\subset U_{i}
. Observe that

(IV.2)

\displaystyle\|\psi_{c,\rho}^{\ast}F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C\,\|d\varphi\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\ \|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}\leqslant C\,\|d\varphi\|^{2}_{\infty}\ \sqrt{\varepsilon_{G}}.

We first choose
\varepsilon_{G}>0
in (
IV.1
) such that we can apply Proposition
A.2
to
\,\psi_{c,\rho}^{\ast}(A^{h_{i_{l}}})
on the geodesic ball
O_{l}
. Let
g_{l}\in W^{1,2}(O_{l},G)
given by Proposition
A.2
such that

(IV.3)

\displaystyle B_{l}:=\left(\psi_{c,\rho}^{*}(A^{h_{i_{l}}})\right)^{g_{l}}

satisfies

(IV.4)

\displaystyle\|B_{l}\|_{W^{1,2}(O_{l})}\leqslant C\,\|\psi_{c,\rho}^{\ast}F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C\,\|d\varphi\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\ \|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}

and

(IV.5)

\displaystyle d^{\ast_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}}B_{l}=0.

Using the same argument as in
[
26
, proof of Theorem V.5]
, we have that the transition functions
\sigma_{mn}:=g_{n}^{-1}\,(h_{i_{n}}\circ\psi_{c,\rho}^{-1})^{-1}h_{i_{m}}\circ\psi_{c,\rho}^{-1}\,g_{m}
are continuous on
O_{n}\cap O_{m}
.
and the co-cycle generated by
(\sigma_{mn})_{m,n\in\{1\cdots L\}}
is
C^{0}
approximable by a sequence of smooth ones. Hence,
\sigma_{mn}
are the transition functions of a smooth bundle and
\psi_{c,\rho}^{*}A
defines a
W^{1,2}-
Sobolev connection on this
G-
bundle. Using
[
37
]
, for
\varepsilon_{G}
small enough the bundle is trivial and there exists a global
W^{1,2}
representative of
\psi_{c,\rho}^{*}A
. Pulling back this representative by

\psi_{c,\rho}^{-1}
we have the existence of
g\in W^{1,2}(\partial Q,G)
such that
A^{g}\in L^{4}(\partial Q)
. This concludes the proof of Lemma
IV.1
.
∎

We recall the definition of the Morrey seminorms in
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
:

\displaystyle|f|_{M^{s}_{p,q}(\Omega)}:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\Omega\\
\rho>0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{n-pq}}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\sum_{s_{1}+\cdots+s_{n}=s}\left|\partial^{s_{1}}_{x_{1}}\cdots\partial_{x_{n}}^{s_{n}}f\right|^{p}(x)\,d\mathcal{L}^{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

for every
s\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
1\leqslant p<+\infty
,
0<q<\frac{n}{p}
.

Definition IV.1
.

Given an admissible cubic
\varepsilon
-cover
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}
, we say that a cube
Q\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
is
uniformly transversal
to
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}
if there are universal constants
0<\alpha\leqslant k
such that for every
Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon}
we have that
Q\cap Q^{\prime}
is either empty or a rectangle with minimum edge length
\alpha\varepsilon
and maximum edge length
k\varepsilon
.

Theorem IV.1

(Approximation under controlled traces of the curvature)
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group and let
\Lambda>0
. There exist
\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,1)
and
C_{G}>0
depending only on
G
such that for every
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
satisfying

\displaystyle|F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in Q_{1}^{5}(0)\\
\rho>0\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}<\varepsilon_{G}

we can find a set
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\big(0,\frac{1}{4}\big)
of admissible scales for
A
(with associated admissible cubic
\varepsilon_{i}
-covers
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
) and a family of
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-forms
\{A_{i,\Lambda}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
such that the following facts hold.

(i)

For every
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\in M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
with

\displaystyle|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}<C_{G}\,|F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}.

(ii)

There exist a universal integer
N\in\mathbb{N}
and a non-negative
f\in M^{0}_{1,4}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
satisfying

\displaystyle|f|_{M^{0}_{1,4}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}\leqslant|F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}

such that for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
, for every
x\in Q_{1}^{5}(0)
and for every
\rho>0
for which
Q_{\rho}(x)
is uniformly transversal to
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
and
\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\subset Q_{1-\frac{\varepsilon_{i}}{2}}(0)
we have

(IV.6)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{C_{G}}{\varepsilon_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(x_{k})}f\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

for an
N
-tuple of points
\{x_{k}=x_{k}(i,x,\rho)\}_{k=1,...,N}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
.

Moreover, for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
, for every
x\in Q_{1}^{5}(0)
and for
\mathcal{L}^{1}
-a.e.
\rho>0
for which
Q_{\rho}(x)
is uniformly transversal to
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
and
\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\subset Q_{1-\frac{\varepsilon_{i}}{2}}(0)
we have
\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}\in{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x))
.

(iii)

We have

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}\|A_{i,\Lambda}-A\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\leqslant C_{G}\lim_{i\to+\infty}\int_{\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A}}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

where
\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
is defined as in Lemma
III.2
.

Remark IV.1
.

It is not claimed in Theorem
IV.1
that the elements
A_{i,\Lambda}
are in
{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. It could very well be that this is the case and that it could be checked
out of the construction given in the proof but, since it is not needed in the sequel, this property is left open.

Before proving Theorem
IV.1
, we shall need the following lemma, which will be used at several steps of the proof of Theorem
IV.1
and deserves to be stressed separately.

Lemma IV.2
.

Let
A\in L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
, let
Q_{\rho}(c)\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
and let
p\in[1,+\infty)
and
q\in[1,+\infty]
. Assume moreover that the following facts hold.

(i)

F_{A}:=dA+A\wedge A\in L^{p,q}(Q_{\rho}(c))\cap L^{p,q}(Q_{1}^{5}(0)\smallsetminus Q_{\rho}(c))
.

(ii)

There exists an
L^{2}

1
-form
\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}^{\ast}A
on
\partial Q_{\rho}(c)
such that

(IV.7)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\rho-\varepsilon}^{\rho+\varepsilon}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}\left|D_{r}^{\ast}\iota_{\partial Q_{r}(c)}^{\ast}A-\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(c)}^{\ast}A\right|^{2}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{1}(r)=0,

where
D_{r}(x):=\frac{r}{\rho}\,(x-c)+c
.

Then,
F_{A}:=dA+A\wedge A\in L^{p,q}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))\
.

Proof of Lemma
IV.2
.

Since the proof is identical for
q\neq p
, to fix the ideas we assume that
q=p
, i.e.
L^{p,q}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))=L^{p}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. We first claim that
F_{A}\in L^{1}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. Since
A\in L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
, automatically we have
A\wedge A\in L^{1}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
and it suffices to show that the distributional differential of
A
is a
2
-form in
L^{1}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. We claim that the distributional differential of
A
on
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
is exactly
dA\in L^{1}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. Let
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
be any smooth and compactly supported
3
-form on
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
. Fix any
\varepsilon>0
and let
\eta_{\varepsilon}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
a cut-off function that vanishes on
\partial Q_{\rho}(c)
and satisfies

(IV.8)

\displaystyle\eta_{\varepsilon}\equiv 1\qquad

\displaystyle\mbox{ on }\big(Q_{1}^{5}(0)\smallsetminus Q_{\rho+\varepsilon}(c)\big)\cup Q_{\rho-\varepsilon}(c),

(IV.9)

\displaystyle 0\leqslant\eta_{\varepsilon}\leqslant 1\qquad

\displaystyle\mbox{ on }Q_{1}^{5}(0)

and

(IV.10)

\displaystyle\|d\eta_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}\leqslant\frac{C}{\varepsilon}.

Notice that
\eta_{\varepsilon}\varphi\in W_{0}^{1,1}(Q_{1}^{5}(0)\smallsetminus\partial Q_{\rho}(c))
. Hence,

(IV.11)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}A\wedge d(\eta_{\varepsilon}\varphi)=\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\eta_{\varepsilon}(dA\wedge\varphi).

Moreover,

(IV.12)

\displaystyle d(\eta_{\varepsilon}\varphi)=d\eta_{\varepsilon}\wedge\varphi+\eta_{\varepsilon}d\varphi.

Thus,

(IV.13)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}A\wedge d(\eta_{\varepsilon}\varphi)=\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\eta_{\varepsilon}(A\wedge d\varphi)+\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}A\wedge d\eta_{\varepsilon}\wedge\varphi.

This implies that

(IV.14)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\eta_{\varepsilon}(A\wedge d\varphi)=\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\eta_{\varepsilon}(dA\wedge\varphi)-\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}A\wedge d\eta_{\varepsilon}\wedge\varphi.

By assumption (ii), we have

(IV.15)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}A\wedge d\eta_{\varepsilon}\wedge\varphi\to 0

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Hence, by passing to the limit as
\varepsilon\to 0
in (
IV.14
) we get

(IV.16)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}A\wedge d\varphi=\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}dA\wedge\varphi,

which implies that
dA\in L^{1}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
is the distributional differential of
A
on
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
and our claim follows.

Let
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
a smooth and compactly supported
2
-form on
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
such that
\|\varphi\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}\leqslant 1
. Fix any
\varepsilon>0
and let

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}F_{A}\wedge*\varphi

\displaystyle=\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)\smallsetminus Q_{\rho+\varepsilon}(c)}F_{A}\wedge*\varphi+\int_{Q_{\rho+\varepsilon}(c)\smallsetminus Q_{\rho-\varepsilon}(c)}F_{A}\wedge*\varphi+\int_{Q_{\rho-\varepsilon}(c)}F_{A}\wedge*\varphi

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)\smallsetminus Q_{\rho}(c)}F_{A}\wedge*\varphi+\int_{Q_{\rho+\varepsilon}(c)\smallsetminus Q_{\rho-\varepsilon}(c)}F_{A}\wedge*\varphi+\int_{Q_{\rho}(c)}F_{A}\wedge*\varphi

\displaystyle\leqslant\|F_{A}\|_{L^{p}(Q_{1}^{5}(0)\smallsetminus Q_{\rho}(c))}+\|F_{A}\|_{L^{p}(Q_{\rho}(c))}+\int_{Q_{\rho+\varepsilon}(c)\smallsetminus Q_{\rho-\varepsilon}(c)}F_{A}\wedge*\varphi.

Since we have already shown that
F_{A}\in L^{1}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
, by taking the limit as
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
in the above inequality we have

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}F_{A}\wedge*\varphi

\displaystyle\leqslant\|F_{A}\|_{L^{p}(Q_{1}^{5}(0)\smallsetminus Q_{\rho}(c))}+\|F_{A}\|_{L^{p}(Q_{\rho}(c))}.

By taking the supremum over
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
a smooth and compactly supported
2
-form on
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
such that
\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}\leqslant 1
, we finally get

\displaystyle\|F_{A}\|_{L^{p}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|F_{A}\|_{L^{p}(Q_{1}^{5}(0)\smallsetminus Q_{\rho}(c))}+\|F_{A}\|_{L^{p}(Q_{\rho}(c))}.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
IV.2
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
IV.1
.

Let
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}\subset\big(0,\frac{1}{4}\big)
be a set of admissible scales for
A
with associated admissible cubic
\varepsilon_{i}
-cover
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
. We subdivide the family of cubes
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
into a uniformly bounded number
N
of disjoint subfamilies

(IV.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{1},...,\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{N}.

We denote by
\mathscr{C}^{s}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
the set of centers of the cubes in
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}
. The subfamilies
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}
are chosen such that the union of the cubes in
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{j}
with same centers and radii multiplied by 2 have no intersections with the union of the cubes in
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{k}
with same centers and radii multiplied by 2 whenever
j\neq k
.

We claim that for every
s=0,...,N
we can build a
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}\in L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
and we can choose a family of radii
\rho_{i}^{s}\in\big(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon_{i},2\varepsilon_{i}\big)
for each center
c\in\mathscr{C}^{s}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
in the family of cubes in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}
such that for some constant
C_{s}>0
depending only on
G
we have the following.

(a)

We have

(IV.18)

\displaystyle\forall\,c\in\mathscr{C}^{s}_{\varepsilon_{i}}\quad A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\in{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}(\partial Q_{\rho_{i}^{s}}(c)),

and the estimates

(IV.19)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q^{s}_{\rho_{i,c}}(c)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{C_{s}}{2\varepsilon_{i}}\int_{Q^{s}_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

(IV.20)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q^{s}_{\rho_{i,c}}(c)}\lvert{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{C_{s}}{2\varepsilon_{i}}\int_{Q^{s}_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c)}\lvert{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

(b)

F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\in M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
with

(IV.21)

\displaystyle|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1-3\varepsilon_{i}}(0))}<C_{s}\,|F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}.

(c)

There exist
N_{s}\in\mathbb{N}
and a real-valued, non-negative function
f_{s}\in M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
satisfying

(IV.22)

\displaystyle|f_{s}|_{M^{0}_{1,4}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}\leqslant\ |F_{A}|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}

such that, for every
x\in Q_{1}^{5}(0)
and for every
\rho>0
for which
Q_{\rho}(x)
is uniformly transversal to
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
, we have

(IV.23)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap\Omega_{i}^{s}}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{C_{s}}{\varepsilon_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{s}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(x_{k})}f_{s}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

for a family of
N_{s}
points
\{x_{k}^{s}=x_{k}^{s}(i,x,\rho)\}_{k=1,...,N_{s}}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
, where
\Omega_{i}^{s}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
is the open set given by

(IV.24)

\displaystyle\Omega_{i}^{s}:=\operatorname{int}\bigg(\bigcup_{k=1}^{s}\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{k}}\overline{Q}\bigg).

(d)

It holds that

(IV.25)

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}\|A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}-A\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\leqslant C_{s}\lim_{i\to+\infty}\int_{\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A}}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

where
\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
is as in Lemma
III.2
.

Base of the induction
. For
s=0
, since
\Omega_{i}^{0}=\emptyset
we have that
A_{i,\Lambda}^{0}:=A
satisfies (a), (b), (c) and (d).

Induction step
. Let
s\geqslant 1
. Using one more time Fubini theorem combined with the mean value theorem we adjust the radii
\rho_{i,c}\in\big(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon_{i},2\varepsilon_{i}\big)
in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}
such that
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}
satisfies the properties in (a). From now on, in the induction procedure, the family
{\mathcal{Q}}^{s}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
is fixed.

By assumptions (1) and (2) in Definition
III.3
, if
\varepsilon_{G}>0
is small enough then
\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\partial Q)
satisfies the hypotheses of Corollary
II.1
for every
\Lambda
-good cube
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s,g}
. Moreover, by our choice of the cubic cover (see Lemma
III.1
-(i)), we have that
\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\partial Q)
satisfies the hypotheses of Corollary
II.2
for every
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}
.

For every
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}
, if
Q
is
\Lambda
-good then we let
A_{Q}^{s}
be the
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form given by applying Corollary
II.1
–(ii) to
\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}
. On the other hand, if
Q
is
\Lambda
-bad we let
A_{Q}^{s}
be the
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form given by applying Corollary
II.2
–(ii) to
\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}
.

Let
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}
be defined on
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
by

\displaystyle A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}:=\begin{cases}A_{Q}^{s}&\text{ on }Q\text{ for every }Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s},\\
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

By construction and using Lemma
IV.2
, it is clear that
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s},F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\in L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. First, we show that
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}
satisfies (b). Fix any point
x\in Q_{1}^{5}(0)
and let
\rho\in\left(0,\dist(x,\partial Q_{1}^{5}(0))\right)
. First we assume that
\rho\leqslant\varepsilon_{i}
. Then,
Q_{\rho}(x)
intersects at most
\tilde{N}
cubes in
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}
, say
Q_{1},...,Q_{\tilde{N}}
, with
\tilde{N}\in\mathbb{N}
depending only on the choice of the cubic cover and independent on
i
,
x
and
\rho
. Hence, by Hölder inequality, by the estimates (
II.66
) for the good
Q_{\ell}
s and (
II.96
) for the bad ones for each
A_{Q}^{s}
, as well as (
III.2
) and the inductive assumption (b) on
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1})}

\displaystyle\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{\ell=1}^{\tilde{N}}\left\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q_{\ell})}^{2}=C\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{N}}\lVert F_{A_{Q_{\ell}}^{s}}\rVert_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q_{\ell})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\,\left(\lVert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rVert_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\ell})}^{2}+\varepsilon_{i}^{-2}\lVert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rVert_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\ell})}^{2}\right)+\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\lVert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rVert_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\ell})}^{2}

(IV.26)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\ |F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\leqslant C\ |F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
.

Assume now that
\,\varepsilon_{i}<\rho<1
. Then there exists a universal constant
k>1
such that
Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1})
can be covered with a finite number of cubes
\{Q_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1,...,N_{i,x,\rho}}
in
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}
such that

\displaystyle\bigcup_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x,\rho}}Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})\subset Q_{k\rho}(x).

Notice that now the number of cubes
N_{i,x,\rho}
may depend on
i
,
x
and
\rho
. Then, by the estimates (
II.66
) and (
II.96
) we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1})}

\displaystyle\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x,\rho}}\int_{Q_{\ell}}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x,\rho}}\int_{Q_{\ell}}\lvert F_{A_{Q_{\ell}}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

(IV.27)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\,\varepsilon_{i}^{-4}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q_{\ell}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\ \ \varepsilon_{i}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+\ C\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\,\varepsilon_{i}\,\left(\varepsilon_{i}^{-2}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\right)^{3}+C\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x,\rho}}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. By property (4) in Definition
III.3
, by Remark
III.2
and by inductive hypothesis (a) on
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\varepsilon_{i}^{-4}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q_{\ell}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\ \ \varepsilon_{i}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Lambda^{-1}\,\left|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\right|_{M_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Lambda^{-1}\,\left|F_{A}\right|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. By property (5) in Definition
III.3
and by our choice of
k>0
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant C\Lambda\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}(2\varepsilon_{i})^{5}\leqslant C\Lambda(k\rho)^{5},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. Hence, since by assumption
\rho\in(0,1)
, we have obtained the estimate

(IV.28)

\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\varepsilon_{i}^{-4}\,\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q_{\ell}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\ \ \varepsilon_{i}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant C\,k^{5}\rho\,|F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. By Remark
III.2
and by property (5) in Definition
III.3
, provided
i\geqslant i_{0}
is large enough so that
\varepsilon_{i}<\Lambda^{-3}\,|F_{A}|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\varepsilon_{i}\ \left(\varepsilon_{i}^{-2}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\right)^{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\varepsilon_{i}\,\left(\varepsilon_{i}^{-3}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\right)^{3}

(IV.29)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\varepsilon_{i}(\varepsilon_{i}^{-3}\Lambda\varepsilon_{i}^{5})^{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\,\Lambda^{3}\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\varepsilon_{i}^{5}\leqslant C\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\,\Lambda^{3}\,\leqslant C\rho\ |F_{A}|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. By (
III.2
) and the inductive hypothesis (a) on
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}
, we get

(IV.30)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x\rho}}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}&\leqslant C\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x\rho}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C\int_{Q_{k\rho}(x)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant C\,k\,\rho\ \left|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\right|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\\
&\leqslant C\,k\,\rho\,|F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2},\end{split}

for some constant
C>0
depending only on the choice of the cubic cover. Hence, provided
i\geqslant i_{0}
is sufficiently large, combining (
IV.27
), (
IV.28
), (
IV.29
) and (
IV.30
) we get this time for
\varepsilon_{i}<\rho<1

(IV.31)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1})}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant C\ \left|F_{A}\right|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. Since

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1})}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)\smallsetminus(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1})}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1})}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+\frac{1}{\rho}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

property (b) for
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}
follows by (
IV.31
) and inductive hypothesis b) for
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}
.

Now we turn to show property (c) for
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}
. Notice that

\displaystyle\|A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}-A\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}-A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}+\|A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-A\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}}\int_{Q}\lvert A_{Q}^{s}-A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+\|A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-A\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q}\lvert A_{Q}^{s}-A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+\|A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-A\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\int_{Q}\lvert A_{Q}^{s}-A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\,I_{i,\Lambda,s}+2\,J_{i,\Lambda,s}+K_{i,\Lambda,s}+\|A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-A\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2},

with

\displaystyle I_{i,\Lambda,s}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q}\lvert A_{Q}^{s}-(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

\displaystyle J_{i,\Lambda,s}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

\displaystyle K_{i,\Lambda,s}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\int_{Q}\lvert A_{Q}^{s}-A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

By using (
II.70
) with
\bar{A}=(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q}
for every good cube
Q
, by Lemma
III.1
(in particular (
III.4
)) and by properties (1),(2) in Definition
III.3
, we have

\displaystyle I_{i,\Lambda,s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q}\lvert A_{Q}^{s}-(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+\varepsilon^{3}_{i}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\varepsilon_{i}^{-1}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\|{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q)}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+C\,\varepsilon_{i}^{6+1/2}\ \sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}|^{2}_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}(0))}

\displaystyle\quad+\,C\,\varepsilon_{i}^{6}\ \sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}|^{4}_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}(0))}

(IV.32)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+O(\varepsilon_{i})\ \longrightarrow 0

as
\varepsilon_{i}\to 0^{+}
for a fixed
\Lambda
. Now, we estimate
J_{i,\Lambda,s}
. We write

\displaystyle J_{i,\Lambda,s}

\displaystyle\leqslant 3\,\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-A\rvert^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}+3\,\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q}\left|A-(A)_{Q}\right|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}

(IV.33)

\displaystyle\quad+3\,\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q}\lvert(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q}-(A)_{Q}\rvert^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}

\displaystyle\leqslant 6\,\|A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-A\|^{2}_{L^{2}(Q_{1}(0))}+3\,\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}}\int_{Q}\left|A-(A)_{Q}\right|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

Using the induction hypothesis together with (
III.5
) we obtain that

(IV.34)

\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}J_{i,\Lambda,s}\leqslant C\lim_{i\to+\infty}\int_{\Omega^{s-1}_{i,\Lambda,A}}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

Finally, we bound
K_{i,\Lambda,s}
. Using respectively (
II.76
) together with the induction hypothesis on
(Q_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s},A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})
(
IV.19
) and (
IV.20
), we obtain

\displaystyle K_{i,\Lambda,s}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{s,b}}\left(\int_{Q}\lvert A_{Q}^{s}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+\int_{Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{s,b}}\left(\varepsilon_{i}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}+\varepsilon_{i}^{3}\ \int_{\partial Q}|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}|\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}+\int_{Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{s,b}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+C\,\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\ \sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\ \int_{Q}|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}|\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{s,b}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c_{Q})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+C\,\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\ \int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}|^{2}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}

(IV.35)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{s,b}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c_{Q})}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+\|A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-A\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}+o_{\varepsilon_{i}}(1).

Hence, by Lemma
III.2
and by inductive hypothesis (d) on
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}
, property (d) for
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}
follows.

We are just left to show property (c) for
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}
. Fix any
x\in Q_{1}^{5}(0)
and
\rho>0
such that
Q_{\rho}(x)
is uniformly transversal to
\mathcal{Q}^{l}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
for
l\leqslant s
. Notice that,

\displaystyle\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap\Omega_{i}^{s})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap\Omega_{i}^{s-1})}^{2}+\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1}))}^{2}

(IV.36)

\displaystyle=\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap\Omega_{i}^{s-1})}^{2}+\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1}))}^{2}.

By inductive hypothesis (c) on
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}
, there exists
N_{s-1}\in\mathbb{N}
independent on
x
,
\rho
and
i
such that

(IV.37)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap\Omega_{i}^{s-1}}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{C_{s-1}}{\varepsilon_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{s-1}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(x_{k})}f_{s-1}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

for
C_{s-1}>0
depending only on
G
, for an
N_{s-1}
-tuple
\{x_{k}^{s-1}=x_{k}^{s-1}(x,\rho,i)\}_{k=1,...,N_{s-1}}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
and for a real-valued, non-negative
f_{s-1}\in M^{0}_{1,4}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
independent on
i
such that

\displaystyle|f_{s-1}|_{M^{0}_{1,4}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}\leqslant|F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}.

Hence, in order to prove property (c) for
A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}
, it remains to control
\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\rho}(0)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1}))}^{2}
. Notice that, since
\rho\in\big(\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon_{i},\frac{7}{4}\varepsilon_{i}\big)
, there exists
\hat{N}
depending only on the choice of the cubic cover such that
\partial Q_{\rho}
intersects just
\hat{N}
cubes in
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}
, say
\{Q_{1},...,Q_{\hat{N}}\}
. Then, by construction, we have

\displaystyle F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\mathds{1}_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1})}=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s}}F_{A_{Q}^{s}}\mathds{1}_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap Q}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{\hat{N}}F_{A_{Q_{\ell}}}\mathds{1}_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap Q_{\ell}}.

Thus, by (
II.68
) and (
II.98
) we get

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1})}

\displaystyle\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\sum_{\ell=1}^{\hat{N}}\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap Q_{\ell}}\lvert F_{A_{Q_{\ell}}^{s}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\sum_{\ell=1}^{\hat{N}}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}+C\,\varepsilon_{i}^{-6}\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\left(\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\right)^{3}

(IV.38)

\displaystyle\quad+C\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\varepsilon_{i}^{-4}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q_{\ell}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\ \ \int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}.

Fix any
\ell\in\{1,...,\hat{N}\}
. Notice that we have have chosen
Q^{s}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
so that (
IV.19
) holds. Then, we have

(IV.39)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{C}{\varepsilon_{i}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

for some constant
C>0
independent of
\varepsilon_{i}
. Moreover, by properties (2), (4)and (5) in Definition
III.3
(characterizing good cubes), by Remark
III.2
and by (
IV.20
), we obtain the estimates

(IV.40)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}^{-6}\ \left(\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\right)^{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon_{i}^{-6}\ \left(\varepsilon_{i}^{3+1/2}\,\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\right)^{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon_{i}^{3+3/2}\,|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{6}

(IV.41)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{i}}\int_{Q_{\ell}}\,\varepsilon_{i}^{1/2}\,|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{6}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}

and

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}^{-4}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}-(A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1})_{Q_{\ell}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant\ \Lambda^{-1}\ |F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\,\varepsilon_{i}^{4}\,\Lambda\ |F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s-1}}|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}

(IV.42)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{s-1}}{\varepsilon_{i}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\,|F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

By combining (
IV.38
), (
IV.39
), (
IV.40
) and (
IV.42
), we get

(IV.43)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap(\Omega_{i}^{s}\smallsetminus\Omega_{i}^{s-1})}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{C}{\varepsilon_{i}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\hat{N}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}g_{s}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

with
g_{s}:=\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}+|F_{A}|^{4}_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}+\varepsilon_{i}^{1/2}\,|F_{A}|^{6}_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}
. The required estimate (
IV.23
) for
F_{A_{i,\Lambda}^{s}}
then follows by (
IV.36
), (
IV.37
) and (
IV.43
), letting

\displaystyle f_{s}:=\max\{g_{s},f_{s-1}\}.

Finally, let
A_{i,\Lambda}:=A_{i,\Lambda}^{N}\in L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
. By construction,
F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\in L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
and
A_{i,\Lambda}
satisfies the properties (i) and (iii). Moreover, notice that
\Omega_{i}^{N}\supset Q_{1-\frac{\varepsilon_{i}}{2}}(0)
. In particular,
\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\subset\Omega_{i}^{N}
. Hence, by property (c) for
A_{i,\Lambda}^{N}
, there exists
\tilde{N}\in\mathbb{N}
independent on
x
,
\rho
and
i
such that

(IV.44)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{C}{\varepsilon_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{\tilde{N}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(x_{k})}f\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

for
C>0
depending only on
G
, for an
N
-tuple of points
\{x_{k}=x_{k}(x,\rho,i)\}_{k=1,...,N}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
and for some real-valued, non-negative
f\in M^{0}_{1,4}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
independent on
i
such that

(IV.45)

\displaystyle|f|_{M^{0}_{1,4}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}\leqslant\ |F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}

We are just left to check that, given any
x\in Q_{1}^{5}(0)
, for
\mathcal{L}^{1}
-a.e. choice of
\rho>0
such that
Q_{\rho}(x)
is uniformly transversal to
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
and
\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\subset Q_{1-\frac{\varepsilon_{i}}{2}}(0)
we have
\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x))
. First, note that for
\mathcal{L}^{1}
-a.e.
\rho>0
we have
\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}\in L^{2}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x))
. Moreover, by (
IV.44
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}\lvert F_{\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}\lvert\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}^{*}F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

(IV.46)

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant C\varepsilon_{G},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. By construction, we can find a covering
(R_{l})_{\ell=1,...,N_{\rho,x}}
of
\partial Q_{\rho}(x)
where
R_{\ell}
is a rectangle included in some
Q_{l}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\varepsilon_{i}}^{s(l)}
for some
s(l)\in\{0\cdots N\}
depending on
l
such that

\displaystyle A_{i,\Lambda}=A_{Q_{\ell}}^{s(\ell)}\qquad\mbox{ on }R_{\ell}

By the properties of the extensions on good and bad cubes implying that we have respectively (
II.69
) and (
II.99
), if (
IV.46
) holds there exist
g_{\ell}\in W^{1,2}(R_{\ell},G)
such that

\displaystyle(\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{g_{\ell}}\in L^{4}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x)\cap R_{\ell}).

Then, we can apply Lemma
IV.1
to conclude there exists
g\in W^{1,2}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x),G)
such that

(IV.47)

(\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{g}\in L^{4}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x)).

Hence,
\iota_{\partial Q_{\rho}(x)}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\partial Q_{\rho}(x))
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
IV.1
.
∎

V.
The second smoothification: strong
L^{2}
-approximation by smooth connections

The goal of this section is to prove that any weak connection with curvature having a small Morrey norm is strongly approximable in
L^{2}
by smooth connections with small Morrey norm of the curvature as well.
More precisely the main result obtained in this section is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem V.1

(Smooth approximation under controlled Morrey norm)
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. There exists
\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,1)
such that for every
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
satisfying

\displaystyle|F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}<\varepsilon_{G}

there exists a sequence of
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-forms
\{A_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
such that:

(i)

for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle|F_{A_{i}}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}\leqslant C_{G}\,|F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}.

for some constant
C_{G}>0
depending on
G
;

(ii)

\|A_{i}-A\big\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}\to 0
as
i\to+\infty
.

In order to prove Theorem
V.1
we shall need some preliminary results. Recall that we have defined

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon}:=(\varepsilon\mathbb{Z})^{5}\cap\overline{Q_{1-2\varepsilon}(0)}

for every
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\frac{1}{4}\right)
(see the beginning of Section 3). Under the same assumption and notation of Theorem
IV.1
, for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
and for every
t\in Q_{\frac{\varepsilon_{i}}{8}}(0)
the cubes in the grid
\{Q_{\varepsilon_{i}}(c+t)\}_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}}
are uniformly transverse to the cubes in
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
and such that
\partial Q_{\varepsilon_{i}}(c+t)\subset Q_{1-\frac{\varepsilon_{i}}{2}}(0)
for every
c\in\mathscr{C}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
. Since uniform transversality is stable by small perturbations, there exists
\alpha>0
small enough and independent of
\varepsilon_{i}
so that for every
{r}\in\big((1-\alpha)\varepsilon_{i},(1+\alpha)\varepsilon_{i}\big)
and every
t\in Q_{\frac{{r}}{8}}(0)
the cubes in the grid
\{Q_{{r}}(c+t)\}_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}}}
are still uniformly transverse to the cubes in
\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i}}
and such that
\partial Q_{{r}}(c+t)\subset Q_{1-\frac{\varepsilon_{i}}{2}}(0)
for every
c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}}
.

Lemma V.1

(Choice of an admissible cubic grid)
.

Under the same assumption and notation of Theorem
IV.1
, for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
there exist
{r}_{i}\in\big((1-\alpha)\varepsilon_{i},(1+\alpha)\varepsilon_{i}\big)
and a translation
t_{i}\in Q_{\frac{{r}_{i}}{8}}(0)
for which the following facts hold.

(1)

\iota_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{i})}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{i}))
for every
c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}
.

(2)

There exist
N\in\mathbb{N}
and a real-valued, non-negative
f\in M_{1,4}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
satisfying

|f|_{M^{0}_{1,4}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}\leqslant\,|F_{A}|_{M^{0}_{2,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}

such that for every
c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{i})}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{C_{G}}{\varepsilon_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(x_{k})}f\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

for an
N
-tuple of points
\{x_{k}=x_{k}(i,x,{r}_{i})\}_{k=1,...,N}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
.

(3)

It holds that

(V.1)

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}{r}_{i}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{i})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{i})}\rvert^{2}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}=0,

(V.2)

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}{r}_{i}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{i})}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}-(F_{A_{i,\Lambda}})_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{i})}\rvert^{2}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}=0.

Proof of Lemma
V.1
.

By our assumption on
\alpha
and by Theorem
IV.1
-(ii), arguing exactly as in the proof of
[
5
, Lemma 2.1]
(with
p=2
and
q=1
) or
[
4
, Lemma 3.1]
we can show that there exists a full measure subset
E\subset\big((1-\alpha)\varepsilon_{i},(1+\alpha)\varepsilon_{i}\big)
such that for every
{r}\in E
we have that for a.e. translation
t\in Q_{\frac{{r}}{8}}(0)
1. and 2. in the statement hold. We fix
{r}_{i}\in E
and again, exactly as in the proof of
[
5
, Lemma 2.1]
(with
p=2
and
q=1
) or
[
4
, Lemma 3.1]
we have that

(V.3)

\displaystyle I_{{r}_{i}}

\displaystyle:=\int_{Q_{\frac{{r}_{i}}{8}}(0)}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}=o\big({r}_{i}^{4}\big)

(V.4)

\displaystyle J_{{r}_{i}}

\displaystyle:=\int_{Q_{\frac{{r}_{i}}{8}}(0)}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}-(F_{A_{i,\Lambda}})_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}=o\big({r}_{i}^{4}\big)

as
{r}_{i}\to 0^{+}
. Consider the sets

\displaystyle T_{{r}_{i},K,1}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{t\in Q_{\frac{{r}_{i}}{8}}(0)\mbox{ s.t. }\varphi_{1}(t)\geqslant K\fint_{Q_{\frac{{r}_{i}}{8}}(0)}\varphi_{1}(t)\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg\}

\displaystyle T_{{r}_{i},K,2}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{t\in Q_{\frac{{r}_{i}}{8}}(0)\mbox{ s.t. }\varphi_{2}(t)\geqslant K\fint_{Q_{\frac{{r}_{i}}{8}}(0)}\varphi_{2}(t)\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg\},

with

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}(t)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\rvert^{2}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}\qquad\forall\,t\in Q_{\frac{{r}_{i}}{8}}(0),

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}(t)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}-(F_{A_{i,\Lambda}})_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\rvert^{2}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}\qquad\forall\,t\in Q_{\frac{{r}_{i}}{8}}(0).

By integration on
T_{{r}_{i},K,1}
and
T_{{r}_{i},K,2}
we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{5}(T_{{r}_{i},K,1})

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{{r}_{i}^{5}}{K}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{5}(T_{{r}_{i},K,2})

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{{r}_{i}^{5}}{K}.

Moreover, for every
t\in Q_{\frac{{r}_{i}}{8}}(0)\smallsetminus(T_{{r}_{i},K,1}\cup T_{{r}_{i},K,2})
it holds that

(V.5)

\displaystyle{r}_{i}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\rvert^{2}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle<{r}_{i}K\fint_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(0)}\varphi_{1}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}=K\frac{I_{{r}_{i}}}{{r}_{i}^{4}},

(V.6)

\displaystyle{r}_{i}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}-(F_{A_{i,\Lambda}})_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t)}\rvert^{2}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle<{r}_{i}K\fint_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(0)}\varphi_{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}=K\frac{J_{{r}_{i}}}{{r}_{i}^{4}}.

Now, we notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{5}(T_{{r}_{i},4,1}\cup T_{{r}_{i},4,2})\leqslant\frac{{r}_{i}^{5}}{2},

which implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{5}(T_{{r}_{i},A})\geqslant\frac{{r}_{i}^{5}}{2}>0.

with
T_{{r}_{i}}:=T_{{r}_{i},4,1}^{c}\cap T_{{r}_{i},4,2}^{c}
. Hence, we fix
t_{i}\in T_{{r}_{i}}
. By (
V.3
)-(
V.4
) and (
V.5
)-(
V.6
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{{r}_{i}\to 0^{+}}{r}_{i}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{{r}_{i}})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{{r}_{i}})}\rvert^{2}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{{r}_{i}\to 0^{+}}{r}_{i}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}}\int_{\partial Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{{r}_{i}})}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}-(F_{A_{i,\Lambda}})_{Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{{r}_{i}})}\rvert^{2}\ d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle=0.

The statement follows. This concludes the proof of Lemma
V.1
.
∎

Definition V.1
.

Under the same notation that we have used in the previous Lemma
V.1
, we say that the collection of cubes
\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}:=\{Q_{{r}_{i}}(c+t_{i})\}_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{{r}_{i}}}
is a
admissible cubic
{r}_{i}
-grid
relative to
A_{i,\Lambda}
.

Definition V.2

(Good and bad cubes)
.

Under the same notation that we have used in the previous Lemma
V.1
, given an admissible cubic
{r}_{i}
-grid relative to
A_{i,\Lambda}
we say that
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}
is a
\Lambda
-
good
cube if all the following conditions hold:

(1)

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{{r}_{i}^{3}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant{r}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}}
,

(2)
