\displaystyle{\frac{1}{{r}_{i}^{3}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant{r}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}}
,

(3)

\displaystyle{\int_{\partial Q}\lvert{A_{i,\Lambda}}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\frac{1}{{r}_{i}}\int_{Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}}
,

(4)

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{{r}_{i}^{4}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant\Lambda^{-1}\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}}
,

(5)

\displaystyle{\fint_{Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant\Lambda}
.

Otherwise, we say that that
Q
is a
\Lambda
-
bad
cube. We denote by
\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{g}
the set of all the
\Lambda
-good cubes and
\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{b}:=\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}\smallsetminus\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{g}
.

Lemma V.2
.

Under the same notation that we have used in the previous Lemma
V.1
, let
\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}
be a good
{r}_{i}
-grid relative to
A_{i,\Lambda}
. We have

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\int_{Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant C\lim_{i\to+\infty}\int_{\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A_{i,\Lambda}}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

where
\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A_{i,\Lambda}}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
is given by

\displaystyle\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A_{i,\Lambda}}:=\bigcup\bigg\{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}\mbox{ s.t. }\fint_{Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}>\Lambda\bigg\}.

Proof of Lemma
V.2
.

The proof is identical to the one of Lemma
III.2
.
∎

Remark V.1
.

Notice that we have

\displaystyle r_{i}\int_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant r_{i}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{i},\Lambda}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+r_{i}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant r_{i}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{i},\Lambda}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\int_{Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}.

Thus, by (
V.1
) and by Lemma
V.2
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}r_{i}\int_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{r_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\int_{\partial Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant C\lim_{i\to+\infty}\int_{\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A_{i,\Lambda}}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

where
\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A_{i,\Lambda}}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
is given as in Lemma
V.2
.

We now have all the tools that are needed to prove the following strong
L^{2}
-approximation result by smooth connections under controlled Morrey norm of their curvatures.

Proof of Theorem
V.1
.

We assume that
\varepsilon_{G}>0
is small enough so that we can apply Theorem
IV.1
to
A
. Hence, we find a sequence of admissible scales
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
\varepsilon_{i}\to 0^{+}
as
i\to+\infty
and a sequence
\{A_{i,\Lambda\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}
satisfying (i), (ii) and (iii) in Theorem
IV.1
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}
be an admissible cubic
{r}_{i}
-grid relative to
A_{i,\Lambda}
in the sense of Definition
V.1
. Recall that by definition
{r}_{i}\in\big((1-\alpha)\varepsilon_{i},(1+\alpha)\varepsilon_{i}\big)
, for some fixed
\alpha\in\big(0,\frac{1}{2}\big)
sufficiently small. We fix
i\in\mathbb{N}
big enough so that

\displaystyle\overline{Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}\subset\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}}Q.

Step 1: construction of the approximating
1
-forms.

Let
N_{i}
be the number of cubes in
\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}
. We first enumerate the family

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}=\big\{Q_{1},...,Q_{N_{i}}\big\}

in such a way that for every for every
n\in\{1,...,N_{i}\}
we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{i}^{n}:=\operatorname{int}\bigg(\bigcup_{k=1}^{n}\overline{Q_{k}}\bigg).

satisfies
\emptyset\neq H_{i}^{n}:=\partial\Omega_{i}^{n}\cap\partial Q_{n+1}\neq\partial Q_{n+1}
and
\Omega_{i}^{n}
is bi-Lipschitz equivalent to a
5
-dimensional ball, for every
n=1,...,N_{i}
.
14
14

14

Notice that, by construction,
H_{n}^{i}
is always non-empty and bi-Lipschitz equivalent to a
4
-dimensional closed ball.

If
\varepsilon_{G}>0
is small enough, by 1. and 2. in Lemma
V.1
we have that
\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}
satisfies the assumptions of Corollary
II.2
on
\partial Q
, for every
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}
. Moreover, if
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}
is
\Lambda
-good then
\iota_{\partial Q}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}
satisfies the assumptions of Corollary
II.1
on
\partial Q
. For every
k\in\{1,...,N_{i}\}
we denote by
A_{Q_{k}}\in L^{5}(Q_{k})
the extension given by applying Corollary
II.1
–(i) (if
Q_{k}
is
\Lambda
-good) or Corollary
II.2
–(i) (if
Q_{k}
is
\Lambda
-bad) to
\iota_{\partial Q_{k}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}
.
Hence, for every
k\in\{1,...,N_{i}\}
such that
Q_{k}
is
\Lambda
-good there exists
g_{k}\in W^{1,2}(\partial Q_{k},G)
such that for every
4
-dimensional face
F
of
\partial Q_{k}
we have

(V.7)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\|(\iota_{\partial Q_{k}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{g_{k}}\|_{W^{1,2}(F)}\leqslant C\big(\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{k})}+{r}_{i}^{-1}\|A_{i,\Lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{k})}\big)\\
\iota_{F}^{*}A_{Q_{k}}=(\iota_{F}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{g_{Q_{k}}}\end{cases}

and the estimates

(V.8)

\displaystyle\|F_{A_{Q_{k}}}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{k})}^{2}+{r}_{i}^{-2}\|A_{i,\Lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{k})}^{2}\|A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q_{k}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{r}_{i}^{-4}\|A_{i,\Lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{k})}^{6})

(V.9)

\displaystyle\|A_{Q_{k}}-\bar{A}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\big({r}_{i}\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{k})}^{2}+{r}_{i}^{-1}\|A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q_{k}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{k})}^{2}+{r}_{i}^{-3}\|A_{i,\Lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{k})}^{2}\big)

for every constant
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form
\bar{A}
on
\mathbb{R}^{5}
, where
C>0
is a constant depending only on
G
. On the other hand, for every
k\in\{1,...,N_{i}\}
such that
Q_{k}
is
\Lambda
-bad there exists
g_{k}\in W^{1,2}(\partial Q_{k},G)
such that for every
4
-dimensional face
F
of
\partial Q_{k}
we have

(V.10)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\|(\iota_{\partial Q_{k}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{g_{k}}\|_{W^{1,2}(F)}\leqslant C\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{k})}\\
\iota_{F}^{*}A_{Q_{k}}=(\iota_{F}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{g_{Q_{k}}},\end{cases}

and the estimate

(V.11)

\displaystyle\|F_{A_{Q_{k}}}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\|_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{k})}^{2},

where
C>0
is again a constant depending only on
G
. Lastly, for every
k=1,...,N_{i}
, let
\tilde{g}_{k}\in W^{1,2}(Q_{k},G)
be the extension of
g_{k}
given by Corollary
II.1
-(ii), if
Q_{k}
is
\Lambda
-good, or Corollary
II.2
-(ii), if
Q_{k}
is
\Lambda
-bad.

In order to construct the approximating 1-forms, we proceed by induction on
n=1,...,N_{i}
. In particular, for every
n=1,...,N_{i}
we build a
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form
\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{n}\in L^{5,\infty}(\Omega_{i}^{n})
with
F_{\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{n}}\in L^{\frac{5}{2},\infty}(\Omega_{i}^{n})
such that there exists a gauge transformation
\sigma_{n}\in W^{1,2}(\Omega_{i}^{n},G)\cap W^{1,2}(\partial\Omega_{i}^{n},G)
satisfying

\displaystyle\iota_{\partial\Omega_{i}^{n}}^{*}\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{n}=(\iota_{\partial\Omega_{i}^{n}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{\sigma_{n}}\in L^{4}(\partial\Omega_{i}^{n})

Base of the induction
. At the initial step
n=1
, we have
\Omega_{i}^{1}=Q_{1}
and we set
\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{0}:=A_{Q_{1}}\in L^{5}(\Omega_{i}^{1})
. By (
V.8
) (if
Q_{1}
is
\Lambda
-good) or (
V.11
) (if
Q_{1}
is
\Lambda
-bad) we get
F_{\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{1}}\in L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\Omega_{i}^{1})
. Let

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}:=\tilde{g}_{1}\in W^{1,2}(\Omega_{i}^{1},G)\cap W^{1,2}(\partial\Omega_{i}^{1},G),

we have

\displaystyle\iota_{\partial\Omega_{i}^{1}}^{*}\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{1}=\iota_{\partial\Omega_{i}^{1}}^{*}A_{Q_{1}}=(\iota_{\partial\Omega_{i}^{1}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{g_{1}}=(\iota_{\partial\Omega_{i}^{1}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{\sigma_{1}}.

Hence, we have proved our claim for
n=1
.

Induction step
. Let
\eta_{n}:=g_{n}^{-1}\sigma_{n-1}\in W^{1,4}(H_{i}^{n-1},G)
and notice that, by the inductive assumption, we have

(V.12)

\displaystyle\big((\iota_{H_{i}^{n-1}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{g_{n}}\big)^{\eta_{n}}=(\iota_{H_{i}^{n-1}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{\sigma_{n-1}}=\iota_{H_{i}^{n-1}}^{*}\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{n-1}\in L^{4}(H_{i}^{n-1}).

Hence, we have

(V.13)

\displaystyle d\eta_{n}=\eta_{n}\big(\iota_{H_{i}^{n-1}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda}\big)^{g_{n}}-\big(\iota_{H_{i}^{n-1}}^{*}\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{n-1}\big)\eta_{n}\in L^{4}(H_{i}^{n-1}).

Thus, by Corollary
II.3
, if
\varepsilon_{G}>0
is small enough we can find an extension
h_{n}\in W^{1,(5,\infty)}(Q_{n},G)
of
\eta_{n}
. Let
\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{n}\in L^{5,\infty}(\Omega_{i}^{n})
and
\sigma_{n}\in W^{1,2}(\Omega_{i}^{n},G)\cap W^{1,2}(\partial\Omega_{i}^{n},G)
be given by

(V.14)

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{n}:=\begin{cases}\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{n-1}&\mbox{ on }\Omega_{i}^{n-1}\\
(A_{Q_{n}})^{h_{n}}&\mbox{ on }Q_{n}\end{cases}

and

(V.15)

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}:=\begin{cases}\sigma_{n-1}&\mbox{ on }\Omega_{i}^{n-1}\\
\tilde{g}_{n}h_{n}&\mbox{ on }Q_{n}.\end{cases}

By construction and by Lemma
IV.2
, we have
F_{\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{n}}\in L^{\frac{5}{2},\infty}(\Omega_{i}^{n})
. Moreover, since
\partial\Omega_{i}^{n}\smallsetminus\partial\Omega_{i}^{n-1}\subset\partial Q_{n}
and we have

\displaystyle\iota_{H_{i}^{n}}^{*}\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{n}=\iota_{H_{i}^{n}}^{*}(A_{Q_{n}}^{h_{n}})=(\iota_{H_{i}^{n}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{g_{n}h_{n}}=(\iota_{H_{i}^{n}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{\sigma_{n}},

we conclude that

\displaystyle\iota_{\partial\Omega_{i}^{n}}^{*}\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{n}=(\iota_{\partial\Omega_{i}^{n}}^{*}A_{i,\Lambda})^{\sigma_{n}}\in L^{4}(\partial\Omega_{i}^{n}).

This concludes the proof of the induction step and of our claim at once.

Now, to lighten the notation let
\Omega_{i}:=\Omega_{i}^{N_{i}}
and define

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}:=\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{N_{i}}\in L^{5,\infty}(\Omega_{i}).

\displaystyle\sigma_{i,\Lambda}:=\sigma_{N_{i}}\in W^{1,2}(\Omega_{i},G).

Step 2: Morrey norm control on the curvatures.

Note that, by construction, we have
\overline{Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}\subset\Omega_{i}
. Fix any point
x\in Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)
and let
{r}\in\big(0,\dist(x,\partial Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))\big)
. Assume that
{r}\leqslant\varepsilon_{i}
. Then,
Q_{{r}}(x)
intersects at most
\tilde{N}
cubes in
\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}^{s}
, say
Q_{1},...,Q_{\tilde{N}}
, with
\tilde{N}\in\mathbb{N}
depending only on the choice of the cubic cover. Hence, by Hölder inequality, by the estimates (
II.65
), (
II.95
) for each
A_{Q}
, by the property (1) of
\Lambda
-good cubes and by the property (i) of
A_{i,\Lambda}
given by Theorem
IV.1
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{r}}\int_{Q_{{r}}(x)}\lvert F_{\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|F_{\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q_{{r}}(x))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{\ell=1}^{\tilde{N}}\lVert F_{\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}}\rVert_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q_{\ell})}^{2}=C\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{N}}\lVert F_{A_{Q_{\ell}}}\rVert_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(Q_{\ell})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\big(\lVert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rVert_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\ell})}^{2}+\varepsilon_{i}^{-2}\lVert A_{i,\Lambda}\rVert_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\ell})}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{b}}\lVert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rVert_{L^{2}(\partial Q_{\ell})}^{2}

\displaystyle<C\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\leqslant C\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
.

Assume now that
\,\varepsilon_{i}<{r}<1
. Then there exists a universal constant
k>1
such that
Q_{{r}}(x)
can be covered with a finite number of cubes
\{Q_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1,...,N_{i,x,{r}}}
in
\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}^{s}
such that

\displaystyle\bigcup_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x,{r}}}Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})\subset Q_{k{r}}(x).

Notice that now the number of cubes
N_{i,x,{r}}
may depend on
i
,
x
and
{r}
. Then, by the estimates (
II.66
) and (
II.96
) we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{{r}}(x)}

\displaystyle\lvert F_{\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x,{r}}}\int_{Q_{\ell}}\lvert F_{\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x,{r}}}\int_{Q_{\ell}}\lvert F_{A_{Q_{\ell}}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\bigg(\varepsilon_{i}^{-4}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q_{\ell}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)\bigg(\varepsilon_{i}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon_{i}\bigg(\varepsilon_{i}^{-2}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)^{3}\bigg)+C\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x,{r}}}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. By property (4) in Definition
V.2
and by the property (i) of
A_{i,\Lambda}
given by Theorem
IV.1
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}

\displaystyle\bigg(\bigg(\varepsilon_{i}^{-4}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q_{\ell}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)\bigg(\varepsilon_{i}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Lambda^{-1}[F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}]_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Lambda^{-1}\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. By property (5) in Definition
V.2
and by our choice of
k>0
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant C\Lambda\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}(2\varepsilon_{i})^{5}\leqslant C\Lambda(k{r})^{5},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. Hence, since by assumption
{r}\in(0,1)
, we have obtained the estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\bigg(\bigg(\varepsilon_{i}^{-4}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}-(A_{i,\Lambda})_{Q_{\ell}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)\bigg(\varepsilon_{i}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)\leqslant Ck^{5}{r}\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. By property (5) in Definition
V.2
, provided
i\geqslant i_{0}
is large enough so that
\varepsilon_{i}<\Lambda^{-3}\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}
we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\varepsilon_{i}\bigg(\varepsilon_{i}^{-2}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)^{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\varepsilon_{i}\bigg(\varepsilon_{i}^{-3}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg)^{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\varepsilon_{i}(\varepsilon_{i}^{-3}\Lambda\varepsilon_{i}^{5})^{3}\leqslant\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\Lambda^{3}\sum_{Q_{\ell}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\varepsilon_{i},\Lambda}^{g}}\varepsilon_{i}^{5}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\Lambda^{3}\leqslant C{r}\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
. By Lemma
V.1
–(ii) and by the property (i) of
A_{i,\Lambda}
given by Theorem
IV.1
we get

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x{r}}}\int_{\partial Q_{\ell}}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{i,x,{r}}}\int_{Q_{2\varepsilon_{i}}(c_{Q_{\ell}})}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{Q_{k{r}}(x)}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant Ck{r}\lvert F_{A_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ck{r}\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on the choice of the cubic cover. Hence, provided
i\geqslant i_{0}
i sufficiently large we get

(V.16)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{r}}\int_{Q_{{r}}(x)}\lvert F_{\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\leqslant C\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2},

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
G
.

Step 3: strong
L^{2}
-convergence of the connections.

Notice that, by construction, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\tilde{A}_{i,\Lambda}^{\sigma_{i,\Lambda}^{-1}}-A\Big\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{i})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}\Big\|A_{Q_{n}}^{\tilde{g}_{n}^{-1}}-A\Big\|_{L^{2}(Q_{n})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}\Big\|A_{Q_{n}}^{\tilde{g}_{n}^{-1}}-A_{i,\Lambda}\Big\|_{L^{2}(Q_{n})}^{2}+\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}\big\|A_{i,\Lambda}-A\big\|_{L^{2}(Q_{n})}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}\Big\|A_{Q_{n}}^{\tilde{g}_{n}^{-1}}-A_{i,\Lambda}\Big\|_{L^{2}(Q_{n})}^{2}+\big\|A_{i,\Lambda}-A\big\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}(0))}^{2}\bigg)

for some constant
C>0
depending on
G
and
A
. By the same procedure that we have used in the proof of Theorem
IV.1
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to+\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}\Big\|A_{Q_{n}}^{\tilde{g}_{n}^{-1}}-A_{i,\Lambda}\Big\|_{L^{2}(Q_{n})}\leqslant C\lim_{i\to+\infty}\int_{\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A_{i,\Lambda}}}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

where
C>0
is a universal constant depending only on
G
and
\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A_{i,\Lambda}}\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
is given as in Lemma
V.2
.
Now we notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{5}(\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A_{i,\Lambda}})

\displaystyle={r}_{i}^{5}\operatorname{card}\bigg(\bigg\{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}\mbox{ s.t. }\fint_{Q}\lvert A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,dx^{5}>\Lambda\bigg\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leqslant{r}_{i}^{5}\operatorname{card}\bigg(\bigg\{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{{r}_{i}}\mbox{ s.t. }\fint_{Q}\lvert A-A_{i,\Lambda}\rvert^{2}\,dx^{5}+\fint_{Q}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}>\Lambda\bigg\}\bigg)\leqslant\frac{C}{\Lambda}

and, recalling the definition of
\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A}
from Lemma
III.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{5}(\Omega_{i,\Lambda,A})\leqslant\frac{C}{\Lambda}

for every given
i
, where
C>0
is independent on
i
. Thus, by Theorem
IV.1
-(iii), we get that for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
i(j)>0
big enough so that be letting
\tilde{A}_{j}:=\tilde{A}_{i(j),j}
and
\sigma_{j}:=\sigma_{i(j),j}^{-1}\in W^{1,2}(\Omega_{i(j)})
we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\tilde{A}_{j}^{\sigma_{j}^{-1}}-A_{i(j),j}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{i(j)})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{\Omega_{i(j),j,A_{i(j),j}}}\lvert A_{i(j),j}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bigg(\int_{\Omega_{i(j),j,A_{i(j),j}}}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert A_{i(j),j}-A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{\Omega_{i(j),j,A_{i(j),j}}}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}+\int_{\Omega_{i(j),j,A}}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5},

where
C>0
doesn’t depend on
j
. Finally, we get

\displaystyle\Big\|\tilde{A}_{j}^{\sigma_{j}^{-1}}-A\Big\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{i(j)})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{\Omega_{i(j),j,A_{i(j),j}}}\lvert A\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}\to 0

as
j\to+\infty
.

Notice that, by assumption,
\Omega_{i(j)}\supset\overline{Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}
for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
\pi_{2}(G)=0
, we can find a sequence
\{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C^{\infty}\big(\overline{Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}\big)
such that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\sigma}_{j}-\sigma_{j}\|_{W^{1,2}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}\rightarrow 0

and
\tilde{\sigma}_{j}-\sigma_{j}\to 0
pointwise
\mathcal{L}^{5}
-a.e. on
Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)
as
j\to+\infty
. Moreover, since we have the improved integrability properties
A\in L^{5,\infty}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))
and
F_{A}\in L^{\frac{5}{2},\infty}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))
, by standard convolution with a smooth mollifying kernel we can find a sequence
\{\hat{A}_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)\big)
such that

\displaystyle|F_{\hat{A}_{j}}|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}^{2}\leqslant 2|F_{\tilde{A}_{j}}|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}^{2}\leqslant 2C\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\qquad\forall\,j\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle\|\hat{A}_{j}-\tilde{A}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}\to 0

and
\hat{A}_{j}-\tilde{A}_{j}\to 0
pointwise
\mathcal{L}^{5}
-a.e. on
Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)
as
j\to+\infty
.
Now define

\displaystyle A_{j}:=\hat{A}_{j}^{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{-1}}\in C^{\infty}\big(\overline{Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}\big)\qquad\forall\,j\in\mathbb{N}.

By construction and
\ad
-invariance of the norm on
\mathfrak{g}
, we have

\displaystyle|F_{\tilde{A}_{j}}|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}^{2}\leqslant 2|F_{\hat{A}_{j}}|_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}^{2}\leqslant 2C\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}\qquad\forall\,j\in\mathbb{N}.

By construction and dominated convergence, we have

\displaystyle\|A_{j}-A\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}\leqslant\big\|A_{j}-\tilde{A}_{j}^{\sigma_{j}^{-1}}\big\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}+\big\|\tilde{A}_{j}^{\sigma_{j}^{-1}}-A\big\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}\to 0

as
j\to+\infty
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
V.1
.
∎

VI.
Partial regularity for stationary weak Yang–Mills fields

In this section we prove our main result about the partial regularity of stationary weak Yang–Mills fields on the unit cube
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
. In order to get that, we first use Theorem
V.1
and the main result in
[
17
]
to obtain the following Coulomb gauge extraction theorem for weak connections whose curvature has sufficiently small Morrey norm.

Theorem VI.1

(Coulomb gauge extraction under small Morrey norm assumption)
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. There exists
\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,1)
such that for every weak connection
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
satisfying

\displaystyle\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}<\varepsilon_{G}

there exist
g\in W^{1,2}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0),G)
such that
A^{g}\in(M_{2,2}^{1}\cap M_{4,1}^{0})(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))
satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{cases}d^{*}A^{g}=0,\\
\iota_{\partial Q_{1}^{5}(0)}^{*}(*A^{g})=0,\\
\lvert\nabla A^{g}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}+\lvert A^{g}\rvert_{M_{4,1}^{0}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}\leqslant C_{G}\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))},\end{cases}

for some constant
C_{G}>0
depending only on
G
.

Proof of Theorem
VI.1
.

By Theorem
V.1
, if
\varepsilon_{G}>0
is small enough we can find a sequence of
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-forms
\{A_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))
such that

(1)

for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle\lvert F_{A_{i}}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}^{2}\leqslant K_{G}\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}^{2}.

for some constant
K_{G}>0
depending on
G
;

(2)

\|A_{i}-A\big\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}\to 0
as
i\to+\infty
.

By choosing
\varepsilon_{G}>0
possibly smaller, we can make sure that
\sqrt{K_{G}\varepsilon_{G}}
is smaller than the constant given by
[
17
, Theorem 1.3]
and we can apply such statement to conclude that for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a gauge
g_{i}
such that
d^{*}A_{i}^{g_{i}}=0
on
Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)
,
\iota_{\partial Q_{1}^{5}(0)}^{*}(*A^{g})=0
and

(VI.1)

\displaystyle\lvert\nabla A_{i}^{g_{i}}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}+\lvert A_{i}^{g_{i}}\rvert_{M_{4,1}^{0}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))}\leqslant C_{G}\lvert F_{A_{i}}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))},

for some constant
C_{G}>0
depending only on
G
. By standard Sobolev embedding theorems, there exists a subsequence (not relabeled) such that
A_{i}^{g_{i}}\rightharpoonup\tilde{A}\in M_{4,1}^{0}
weakly in
L^{4}
and
\nabla A_{i}^{g_{i}}\rightharpoonup\nabla\tilde{A}\in M_{2,2}^{0}
weakly in
L^{2}
. Clearly, we have
d^{*}\tilde{A}=0
. Notice that

\displaystyle dg_{i}=g_{i}A_{i}^{g_{i}}-A_{i}g_{i}.

By (
VI.1
) and 1, we get that
\{g_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded in
W^{1,2}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0),G)
. Hence, there exists a subsequence (not relabeled) such that
g_{i}\rightharpoonup g
weakly in
W^{1,2}
. By Sobolev embedding theorems, we have
g_{i}\to g
strongly in
L^{2}
. Thus, since
A_{i}\to A
strongly in
L^{2}
and
G
is bounded we conclude that
A^{g}=\tilde{A}
on
Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
VI.1
.
∎

Definition VI.1

(Weak Yang–Mills connections)
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. We say that
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
is
weak Yang–Mills connection
on
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
if

\displaystyle d_{A}^{*}F_{A}=0\qquad\mbox{ distrubutionally on }Q_{1}^{5}(0),

i.e.

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}F_{A}\cdot d_{A}\varphi=0\quad\mbox{ for every }\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\wedge^{1}Q_{1}^{5}(0)\otimes\mathfrak{g}).

Definition VI.2

(Stationary weak Yang–Mills connections)
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. We say that a weak Yang–Mills connection
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
on
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
is
stationary
if

(VI.2)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\text{YM}(\Phi_{t}^{*}A)=0,

for every smooth
1
-parameter group of diffeomorphisms
\Phi_{t}
of
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
with compact support.

Remark VI.1
.

Note that if
A
is a stationary weak Yang–Mills connection and
\tilde{A}\in W^{1,2}\cap L^{4}
is another
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form on
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
such that
\tilde{A}=A^{g}
for some
g\in W^{1,2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0),G)
, then
\tilde{A}
is a stationary weak Yang–Mills connection as well. The proof of such fact follows by direct computation exploiting the gauge invariance of the Yang–Mills functional.

If
A
is a stationary Yang–Mills connection, it can be shown that the following
monotonicity property
holds true: for every given
x\in Q_{1}^{5}(0)
the function

(VI.3)

\displaystyle\big(0,\dist(x,\partial Q_{1}^{5}(0)\big)\ni\rho\to\frac{e^{c\Lambda\rho}}{\rho}\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}

is non-decreasing, where
c>0
is a universal constant and
\Lambda
depends on
Q_{1}(x)
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\lvert F_{A}\rvert_{M_{2,2}^{0}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))}\leqslant C\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))},

for some constant
C>0
independent on
A
.
Thanks to (
VI.3
) and to Theorem
VI.1
, we can derive the following
\varepsilon
-regularity statement by using the same arguments presented in
[
17
, Section 4]
. This is a crucial initial step in building a regularity theory for
YM
-energy minimizers, following the same path that R. Schoen and K. Uhlenbeck walked in their work on energy-minimizing harmonic maps in general supercritical dimension
n>2
(as documented in
[
27
]
).

Theorem VI.2

(
\varepsilon
-regularity)
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. There exists
\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,1)
such that for every stationary weak Yang–Mills
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
satisfying

\displaystyle\operatorname{YM}(A)=\int_{Q_{1}^{5}(0)}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{5}<\varepsilon_{G}

there exist
g\in W^{1,2}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0),G)
such that
A^{g}\in C^{\infty}(Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))
.

Standard covering arguments (see e.g.
[
13
, Proposition 9.21]
) together with Theorem
VI.2
and (
VI.3
) give the following bound on the singular set of stationary weak Yang–Mills connections, which is the main result of the present paper.

Theorem VI.3
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group and let
A\in\mathfrak{a}_{G}(Q_{1}^{5}(0))
be a stationary weak Yang–Mills connection on
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
. Then

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{1}(\operatorname{Sing}(A))=0,

where
\mathscr{H}^{1}
is the
1
-dimensional Hausdorff measure on
Q_{1}^{5}(0)
and
\operatorname{Sing}(A)\subset Q_{1}^{5}(0)
is the singular set of
A
, given by
\operatorname{Sing}(A):=Q_{1}^{5}(0)\smallsetminus\operatorname{Reg}(A)
where

\displaystyle\operatorname{Reg}(A):=\{x\in Q_{1}^{5}(0)\mbox{ s.t. }\exists\rho>0,g\in W^{1,2}(B_{\rho}(x),G)\mbox{ s.t. }A^{g}\in C^{\infty}(B_{\rho}(x))\}.

Appendix A
Adapted Coulomb gauge extraction statements in critical dimension

Proposition A.1
.

Let
G
be any compact matrix Lie group. There exist constants
\varepsilon_{G},C_{G}>0
depending only on
G
such that for every
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
A\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
on
\mathbb{S}^{4}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}<\varepsilon_{G}

we can find a gauge
g\in W^{1,4}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
satisfying
A^{g}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
,
d^{*}A^{g}=0
and

\displaystyle\|A^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

Proof of Proposition
A.1
.

Let
\pi
be the stereographic projection from
\mathbb{S}^{4}\smallsetminus\{N\}
into
\mathbb{R}^{4}
which is sending the north pole
N
of
\mathbb{S}^{4}
to infinity and which is conformally invariant. Introduce

\displaystyle D:=(\pi^{-1})^{\ast}A

Because of conformal invariance of the Hodge operator on 2-forms one has

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}|F_{D}|^{2}d\mathcal{L}^{4}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\operatorname{tr}(F_{D}\wedge*F_{D})=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(\pi^{-1})^{*}(\operatorname{tr}(F_{A}\wedge*F_{A})

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}\operatorname{tr}(F_{A}\wedge*F_{A})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}|F_{A}|^{2}d\mathscr{H}^{4}<\varepsilon_{0}.

For every
R>0
, on
B_{R}(0)
one chooses the Uhlenbeck Coulomb gauge
D_{R}:=(D)^{g_{R}}
(see
[
35
]
) that satisfies

\displaystyle d^{*}D_{R}

\displaystyle=0

and

\displaystyle\|D_{R}\|_{L^{4}(B_{R}(0))}+\|dD_{R}\|_{L^{2}(B_{R}(0))}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{G}\ \|F_{D_{R}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{R}(0))}\leqslant C_{G}\ \|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}.

What is crucial here is that all the norms involved are scaling invariant and then the constant
C_{G}>0
is independent of
R
.

Consider now on
\pi^{-1}(B_{R}(0))=\mathbb{S}^{4}\smallsetminus B_{\rho_{R}}(N)
where
\rho_{R}\simeq R/(1+R^{2})\rightarrow 0
as
R\rightarrow+\infty
the 1-form

\displaystyle\pi^{\ast}(D_{R})=A^{h_{R}}

where
h_{R}:=g_{R}\circ\pi
. Using the conformal invariance of the
L^{4}
norms on 1-forms as well as the
L^{2}
norms on
2
-forms one has

(A.1)

\displaystyle\|A^{h_{R}}\|_{L^{4}(\pi^{-1}(B_{R}(0))}+\|d(A^{h_{R}})\|_{L^{2}(\pi^{-1}(B_{R}(0))}\leqslant C_{G}\ \|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

There exists obviously a sequence
R_{k}\to+\infty
such that for some
\Omega\in L_{loc}^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4}\smallsetminus\{N\})
we have

\displaystyle A^{h_{R_{k}}}\rightharpoonup\Omega\mbox{ weakly in }L^{4}_{loc}(\mathbb{S}^{4}\smallsetminus\{N\})\quad

and

\displaystyle h_{R_{k}}\rightharpoonup h_{\infty}\mbox{ weakly in }W^{1,4}_{loc}(\mathbb{S}^{4}\smallsetminus\{N\}).

Moreover

\displaystyle d(A^{h_{R_{k}}})\rightharpoonup d\Omega\mbox{ weakly in }L^{2}_{loc}(\mathbb{S}^{4}\smallsetminus\{N\})

By (
A.1
) we have in particular that

(A.2)

\displaystyle\|\Omega\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\Omega=A^{h_{\infty}}\in L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})

Following Uhlenbeck continuity type argument introduced in
[
23
]
and adapted in
[
16
]
to the 4-dimensional case we construct
g\in W^{1,4}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
such that

\displaystyle d^{*}(g^{-1}dg+g^{-1}\Omega g)=0\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\|dg\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C_{G}\,\|\Omega\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}.

Hence we have the existence of
g\in W^{1,4}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
such that
(A^{h_{\infty}})^{g}=\Omega^{g}
satisfies

\displaystyle d^{*}((A^{h_{\infty}})^{g})=0\quad\mbox{ and }\|(A^{h_{\infty}})^{g}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

which gives

\displaystyle\|(A^{h_{\infty}})^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

This proves the existence of a controlled global Coulomb gauge
\tilde{g}:=h_{\infty}g\in W^{1,4}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
under small curvature assumption. This concludes the proof of Proposition
A.1
.
∎

We recall here the main statement that we will use to extract Coulomb gauges in critical dimension. This is an adaptation of the main Theorem in
[
35
]
.

Proposition A.2
.

Let
G
be a compact matrix Lie group. There exist constants
\varepsilon_{G},C_{G}>0
depending only on
G
such that for every
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
A\in L^{4}(\mathbb{B}^{4})
on
\mathbb{B}^{4}
such that

\displaystyle\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}<\varepsilon_{G}

we can find a gauge
g\in W^{1,4}(\mathbb{B}^{4},G)
satisfying
A^{g}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{4})
,
d^{*}A^{g}=0
and

\displaystyle\|A^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant C_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}

Proof of Proposition
A.2
.

Fix any
4<p<8
. For every
\varepsilon,C>0
, we define

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle:=\{A\in L^{p}(\mathbb{B}^{4},{{\wedge}}^{1}\mathbb{B}^{4}\otimes\mathfrak{g})\mbox{ s.t. }dA\in L^{\frac{p}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4},{{\wedge}}^{2}\mathbb{B}^{4}\otimes\mathfrak{g})\}

\displaystyle\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=\{A\in X\mbox{ with }\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}<\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=\{A\in\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}\mbox{ : }\exists\,g\in W_{\operatorname{id}_{G}}^{1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4},G)\mbox{ : }d^{*}A^{g}=0,\|A^{g}\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant C\|F_{A}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\mbox{, }q=4,p\}.

On
X
, we consider the topology induced by the following norm:

\displaystyle\|A\|_{X}:=\|A\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|dA\|_{L^{\frac{p}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})},\qquad\forall\,A\in X.

Notice that
X
is a Banach vector space with respect to such norm.

Claim
. We claim that there exist
\varepsilon,C>0
such that
\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}=\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}
. In order to achieve such result, we prove separately the following facts.

•

\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}
is path-connected
. Notice that
0\in\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}
. Given any
A\in\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}
, we define

\displaystyle A(t):=tA(t\,\cdot\,),\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,1].

Notice that
A(0)=0
and
A(1)=A
. Moreover,

\displaystyle F_{A(t)}=dA(t)+A(t)\wedge A(t)=t^{2}dA(t\,\cdot\,)+t^{2}A(t\,\cdot\,)\wedge A(t\,\cdot\,)=t^{2}F_{A}(t\,\cdot\,)

which implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}\lvert F_{A(t)}\rvert^{\frac{p}{2}}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}t^{p}\lvert F_{A}(t\,\cdot\,)\rvert^{\frac{p}{2}}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}=t^{p-4}\int_{t\mathbb{B}^{4}}\lvert F_{A}|^{\frac{p}{2}}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}

(A.3)

\displaystyle\leqslant t^{p-4}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}\lvert F_{A}|^{\frac{p}{2}}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}<+\infty

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}\lvert F_{A(t)}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}t^{4}\lvert F_{A}(t\,\cdot\,)\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}=\int_{t\mathbb{B}^{4}}\lvert F_{A}|^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}\lvert F_{A}|^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}<\varepsilon.

Hence,
A(t)\in\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}
for every
t\in[0,1]
. At the same time we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}\lvert A(t)\rvert^{p}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}=\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}t^{p}\lvert A(t\,\cdot\,)\rvert^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}\leqslant t^{p-4}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}\lvert A\rvert^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}\to 0

as
t\to 0^{+}
. Hence,
A(t)\to 0
in
L^{p}(\mathbb{B}^{4})
. As a byproduct, we have
A(t)\wedge A(t)\to 0
in
L^{\frac{p}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})
. Moreover, by (
A.3
) we have
F_{A(t)}\to 0
in
L^{\frac{p}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})
. This implies
dA(t)\to 0
in
L^{\frac{p}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})
.

It follows that
[0,1]\ni t\to A(t)
is a continuous path in
\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}
joining
A
and
0
.

•

For every
\varepsilon,C>0
,
\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}
is closed in
\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}
. Let
\{A_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}
be such that
A_{k}\to A\in\mathscr{U}_{\varepsilon}
. We want to show that
A\in\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}
.

Notice that, by assumption, we have
F_{A_{k}}\to F_{A}
strongly in
L^{\frac{p}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})
. Moreover, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
g_{k}\in W_{\operatorname{id}_{G}}^{1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4},G)
such that
d^{*}A_{k}^{g_{k}}=0
and

\|A_{k}^{g_{k}}\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant C\|F_{A_{k}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant C\varepsilon

for
q=p,4
. Since
\{A_{k}^{g_{k}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
W^{1,\frac{p}{2}}
, there exists a subsequence (not relabeled) such that
A_{k}^{g_{k}}\rightharpoonup A_{\infty}
in
W^{1,\frac{p}{2}}
. Hence, for
q=p,4
, we have

\displaystyle\|A_{\infty}\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\|A_{k}^{g_{k}}\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant C\lim_{k\to+\infty}\|F_{A_{k}}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}=C\|F_{A}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}.

Notice that
d^{*}A_{\infty}=0
and that, by Sobolev embedding theorem, we have
A_{k}^{g_{k}}\to A_{\infty}
strongly in
L^{p}
.
Since

(A.4)

\displaystyle dg_{k}=g_{k}A_{k}^{g_{k}}-A_{k}g_{k},

we get that
\{g_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
W_{\operatorname{id}_{G}}^{1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4},G)
. Thus, there exists a subsequence (not relabeled) such that
g_{k}\rightharpoonup g
in
W_{\operatorname{id}_{G}}^{1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4},G)
. This is enough to pass to the limit in (
A.4
) and we get
A_{\infty}=A^{g}
. Since
g
is a gauge satisfying all the required properties for
A
, we conclude that
A\in\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}
.

•

For some choice of
\varepsilon,C>0
,
\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}
is open in
\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}
. Let
A\in\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}
. It is clear that if we find an open neighborhood of its Coulomb gauge
A^{g}
for the topology of
X
in
\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}
, then
A
posses also such a neighborhood. So we can assume right away that
d^{*}A=0
and

\displaystyle\|A\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant C\|F_{A}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}<C\varepsilon.

Notice that, in such a way, automatically we have
A\in W^{1,\frac{p}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})
.

(A.5)

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{U\in W^{1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4},\mathfrak{g})\mbox{ : }\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}U\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}=0\bigg\}.

(A.6)

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{(f,\alpha)\in W^{-1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4},\mathfrak{g})\times W^{1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{4},\wedge^{3}T^{*}\partial\mathbb{B}^{4}\otimes\mathfrak{g})\mbox{ : }\int_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}f\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}=-\int_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{4}}\alpha\bigg\}.

Notice that
Y
and
Z
are Banach spaces, as they are closed subspaces of
W^{1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4},\mathfrak{g})
and of the product
W^{-1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathfrak{g})\times W^{-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{4},\wedge^{3}T^{*}\partial\mathbb{B}^{4}\otimes\mathfrak{g})
respectively. We introduce the map

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{A}:X\times Y\to Z

given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{A}(\omega,U):=\Big(d^{*}\big((A+\omega)^{\operatorname{exp}(U)}\big),\iota_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{n}}^{*}\big(\hskip-1.99168pt*\hskip-1.42262pt(A+\omega)^{\exp(U)}\big)\Big)

By direct computation we can show that
\mathscr{F}_{A}
is a
C^{1}
-map between Banach spaces. The partial derivative
\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0):Y\to Z
is the following linear and continuous operator between Banach spaces:

\displaystyle\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)[V]

\displaystyle=\big(\Delta V+d^{*}(AV-VA),\partial_{r}V\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\Delta V+d^{*}([A,V]),\partial_{r}V\big),\qquad\forall\,V\in Y.

In order to apply the implicit function theorem to
\mathscr{F}_{A}
we need to show that
\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)
is invertible. First we show that it is injective. Indeed, by standard
L^{p}
-theory for the Laplacian and since
A
is in a controlled Coulomb gauge, for every
V\in Y
we get

\displaystyle\|V\|_{Y}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{C}\Big(\|\Delta V\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|\partial_{r}V\|_{W^{-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{4})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{C}\Big(\|\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)[V]\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|d^{*}([A,V])\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{C}\Big(\|\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)[V]\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|[A,V]\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{C}\Big(\|\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)[V]\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|A\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\|V\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\tilde{C}\Big(\|\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)[V]\|_{L^{\frac{p}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+C\varepsilon\|V\|_{W^{1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\Big),

for some constant
\tilde{C}>0
which just depends on
G
. By choosing
\varepsilon,C>0
in such a way that
C\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2\tilde{C}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|V\|_{Y}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\tilde{C}\|\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)[V]\|_{W^{-1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}.

which implies that
\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)
has trivial kernel. Classical Calderon-Zygmund theory asserts that the operator
\mathcal{L}_{0}:Y\to Z
given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}(V):=\Delta V,\qquad\forall\,V\in Y

is invertible and therefore it has zero index. For every
t\in[0,1]
we define the operator
\mathcal{L}_{t}:Y\to Z
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{t}(V):=\Delta V-t(*[*A,dV]),\qquad\forall\,V\in Y.

and we notice that
[0,1]\ni t\to\mathcal{L}_{t}
is a continuous path of bounded operators joining
\mathcal{L}_{0}
and
\mathcal{L}_{1}=\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)
. By continuity of the Fredholm index, we conclude that
\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)
has zero index. This, together with
\ker\big(\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)\big)=0
, implies that
\partial_{U}\mathscr{F}_{A}(0,0)
is invertible. Hence, we can apply the implicit function theorem in order to get that there exist an open neighbourhood
\mathcal{O}
of
0
in
Y
and
\delta>0
such that for every
\omega\in X
such that
\|\omega\|_{X}<\delta
there exists
U_{\omega}\in\mathcal{O}
such that

\displaystyle 0=\mathscr{F}_{A}(\omega,U_{\omega})=d^{*}\big((A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\big),

where we have set
g_{\omega}:=\exp(U_{\omega})\in W^{1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4},G)
. We just need to establish the bounds

\displaystyle\|(A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant C\|F_{A+\omega}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}

for
q=4,p
. Notice that, by triangular inequality and Sobolev embedding theorem, we get

\displaystyle\|(A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}

\displaystyle=\|(A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|d((A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}})\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\hat{C}\big(\|F_{A+\omega}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|(A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\hat{C}\Big(\|F_{A+\omega}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|(A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\|(A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\Big).

for some constant
\hat{C}>0
depending only on
G
. Now we see that

\displaystyle\|(A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant K\|A\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+K\big(\|\omega\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|dg_{\omega}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\big)

\displaystyle\leqslant K\|A\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+K\big(\|\omega\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|dg_{\omega}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\big),

where again
K>0
depends only on
G
. Notice that by possibly reducing
\delta>0
and the size of the neighbourhood
\mathcal{O}
we bring the quantity
\|\omega\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|dg_{\omega}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}
to be arbitrary small. Then, we choose such parameters possibly depending on
A
in such a way that

\displaystyle\|\omega\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+\|dg_{\omega}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant\|A\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}

which implies

\displaystyle\|(A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2K\|A\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant 2KC\varepsilon

and finally

\displaystyle\|(A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\hat{C}\|F_{A+\omega}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}+2\hat{C}KC\varepsilon\|(A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\Big).

Assuming that
\varepsilon,C>0
are such that
2\hat{C}KC\varepsilon\leqslant\frac{1}{2}
and
C\geqslant 2\hat{C}
we get

\displaystyle\|(A+\omega)^{g_{\omega}}\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|F_{A+\omega}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}

and it follows that

\displaystyle B_{\delta}^{X}(A):=\big\{A+\omega\mbox{ : }\|\omega\|_{X}<\delta\big\}\subset\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}.

This concludes the proof of the openness of
\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}
in
\mathcal{U}_{\varepsilon}
and our claim follows.

Now that the previous claim is proved, we proceed by approximation in the following way. Let
A
satisfy the hypothesis of the statement and let
\{A_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{4})
be such that
A_{k}\to A
strongly in
L^{4}
and
dA_{k}\to dA
strongly in
L^{2}
. Hence, we have that
F_{A_{k}}\to F_{A}
strongly in
L^{2}
and, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
large enough, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}^{4}}\lvert F_{A_{k}}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{4}<\varepsilon.

Hence, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
large enough we have
A_{k}\in\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}
. We choose
\varepsilon,C>0
so that
\mathscr{V}_{C}^{\varepsilon}=\mathscr{U}^{\varepsilon}
. Thus, we get that for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
large enough there exists
g_{k}\in W^{1,p}(\mathbb{B}^{4},G)
such that
d^{*}A_{k}^{g_{k}}=0
and

\displaystyle\|A_{k}^{g_{k}}\|_{W^{1,\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant C\|F_{A_{k}}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}

for
q=4,p
. Hence we find a subsequence (not relabeled) such that
A_{k}^{g_{k}}\rightharpoonup A_{\infty}
weakly in
W^{1,\frac{p}{2}}
. Clearly, we have
d^{*}A_{\infty}=0
. Moreover,
A_{k}^{g_{k}}\rightharpoonup A_{\infty}
weakly in
W^{1,2}
and we get

\displaystyle\|A_{\infty}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\|A_{k}^{g_{k}}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}\leqslant C\lim_{k\to+\infty}\|F_{A_{k}}\|_{L^{2}}=C\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{B}^{4})}.

Since
p>4
, the weak convergence of
A_{k}^{g_{k}}
in
W^{1,\frac{p}{2}}
implies that
A_{k}^{g_{k}}\to A_{\infty}
strongly in
L^{4}
. In particular, by

\displaystyle dg_{k}=g_{k}A_{k}^{g_{k}}-A_{k}g_{k},

we get that
\{g_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded in
W^{1,4}(\mathbb{B}^{4})
. Thus, there exists a subsequence (not relabeled) such that
g_{k}\rightharpoonup g\in W^{1,4}(\mathbb{B}^{4})
weakly in
W^{1,4}
. By passing to the limit in the previous equality, we eventually get

\displaystyle dg=gA_{\infty}-Ag,

i.e.
A_{\infty}=A^{g}
. This concludes the proof of Proposition
A.2
.
∎

Remark A.1
.

A statement that is completely equivalent to Proposition
A.2
also applies to every open and bounded domain
D
contained in
\mathbb{R}^{4}
with a sufficiently smooth boundary. This is necessary to use the standard tools from elliptic regularity theory in the proof. For the purposes of this paper, it suffices to note that all the elliptic regularity theory can be applied to cubes using standard reflection methods. As a result, Proposition
A.2
can be extended to open 4-cubes in
\mathbb{R}^{4}
.

Finally we extend Proposition
A.2
to the sphere case following the same proof of Proposition
A.1
.

Proposition A.3
.

Let
G
be any compact and connected Lie group. There exist constants
\varepsilon_{G},C_{G}>0
depending only on
G
such that for every
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
A\in L^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
on
\mathbb{S}^{4}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}\lvert F_{A}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}<\varepsilon_{G}

we can find a gauge
g\in W^{1,4}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
satisfying
A^{g}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})
,
d^{*}A^{g}=0
and

\displaystyle\|A^{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}\leqslant C_{G}\|F_{A}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}.

Appendix B
G
-valued map extensions of traces in
W^{1,2}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{5},G)

The aim of the present appendix is to show the following extension result for general compact Lie groups.

Proposition B.1
.

Let
G
be a compact Lie group. We can find a smooth Riemannian manifold
M_{G}
such that for every
g\in W^{1,2}(\partial\mathbb{B}^{5},G)
there exists a measurable map

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}(g):\,\,

\displaystyle M_{G}\longrightarrow W^{1,\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5},G)

\displaystyle\quad p\longmapsto\,g_{p}

such that for
\vol_{M_{G}}
-a.e.
p\in M_{G}
we have

(B.1)

\displaystyle g_{p}=g\quad\mbox{ on }\,\mathbb{S}^{4}=\partial\mathbb{B}^{5}

and for
g\equiv g_{0}\in G

(B.2)

g_{p}\equiv g_{0}

We have

(B.3)

\int_{M_{G}}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|d_{x}g_{p}|^{\frac{5}{2}}d\mathcal{L}^{5}\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\leqslant\|dg\|^{\frac{5}{2}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

and

(B.4)

\displaystyle\int_{M_{G}}\bigg(\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert d_{x}g_{p}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\leqslant C_{G}(\Omega)\ \|dg\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

for every
C^{2}
-domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
of
\mathbb{R}^{5}
.

Moreover, for every
C^{2}
-domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
of
\mathbb{R}^{5}
,
\operatorname{Ext}
is continuous from
H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
into
L^{2}(\partial\Omega\times M_{G})
. More precisely, for every pair of maps
(g^{1},g^{2})\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)\times H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
we have

(B.5)

\int_{M_{G}}\bigg(\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert g^{1}_{p}-g^{2}_{p}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\leqslant C_{G}(\Omega)\ \|g^{1}-g^{2}\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

where
C_{G}(\Omega)
only depend on
G
and the
C^{2}
-norm of the domain
\Omega
. In particular for any
g\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
there holds

(B.6)

\displaystyle\int_{M_{G}}\bigg(\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert g_{p}-\operatorname{id}_{G}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)\,d\vol_{M_{G}}(p)\leqslant C_{G}(\Omega)\|g-\operatorname{id}_{G}\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

where
C_{G}(\Omega)
only depend on
G
and the
C^{2}
-norm of the domain
\Omega
.

Remark B.1
.

Notice that, for every compact Lie group, we can write
G
is

(B.7)

\displaystyle G=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{n}G_{i}

where the
G_{i}
are connected, compact Lie groups themselves and the one above is a disjoint union. Hence, up to working on each connected component
G_{i}
of
G
separately, without losing generality we can assume that
G
is compact and connected in the statement of Proposition
B.1
.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
be Lie groups. Assume that we have proved Proposition
B.1
for

G_{1}
and
G_{2}
. Then, Proposition
B.1
holds also for
G_{1}\times G_{2}
with
M_{G_{1}\times G_{2}}:=M_{G_{1}}\times M_{G_{2}}
.

Proof.

Let
g=(g^{1},g^{2})\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G_{1}\times G_{2})
. Applying Proposition
B.1
to
g^{1}
and
g^{2}
separately we find the two maps

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}(g^{1}):\,M_{G_{1}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow W^{1,\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5},G_{1})

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\,g_{p_{1}}^{1}

and

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}(g^{2}):\,M_{G_{2}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow W^{1,\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5},G_{2})

\displaystyle p_{2}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\,g_{p_{2}}^{2}

and we define

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}(g):\,M_{G_{1}\times G_{2}}:=M_{G_{1}}\times M_{G_{2}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow W^{1,\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5},G_{1}\times G_{2})

\displaystyle p:=(p_{1},p_{2})

\displaystyle\longmapsto\,g_{p}:=(g_{p_{1}}^{1},g_{p_{2}}^{2}).

Straightforward computations show that
\operatorname{Ext}(g)
satisfies (
B.1
), (
B.4
) and (
B.6
).
∎

Lemma B.2
.

Let
G,H
be Lie groups and let
\pi:G\to H
be a smooth covering map. Assume that we have proved Proposition
B.1
for
G
. Then, Proposition
B.1
holds also for
H
with
M_{H}:=M_{G}
.

Proof.

Let
g\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},H)
. By
[
2
, Theorem 1]
, we can find a lift
\tilde{g}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
such that
\pi\circ\tilde{g}=g
. Now we use Proposition
B.1
to find the map

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}(\tilde{g}):\,M_{G}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{5},G)

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle\longmapsto\,\tilde{g}_{p}

We define

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}(g):\,M_{G}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{5},H)

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle\longmapsto\,g_{p}:=\pi\circ\tilde{g}_{p}.

Straightforward computations show that
\operatorname{Ext}(g)
satisfies (
B.1
), (
B.4
) and (
B.6
).
∎

We recall the following structure theorem for compact Lie groups, whose proof can be found in
[
29
, §5.2.2 and §5.2.4]
.

Theorem B.1
.

Let
G
be a compact and connected Lie group. Then,
G
is diffeomorphic
(G^{\prime}\times\mathbb{T}^{n})/F
, where:

•

G^{\prime}
is a simply connected compact Lie group.

•

\mathbb{T}^{n}=U(1)\times...\times U(1)
is an
n
-dimensional torus, called the abelian component of
G
.

•

F
is a finite abelian normal subgroup of
G^{\prime}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
.

Recall that if
H
is a discrete normal subgroup of
G
, then the projection map
\pi:G\to G/H
is a covering map. Hence, by Lemma
B.1
and Lemma
B.2
, Theorem
B.1
implies that if we can show Proposition
B.1
for any simply connected compact Lie group and for the group
U(1)
, then we have proved it for every compact and connected Lie group.

We start by facing the case
G=U(1)
.

Proposition B.2
.

Proposition
B.1
holds for
G=U(1)
.

Proof.

Let
g\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},U(1))
. For every
u=u_{1}+iu_{2}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},\mathbb{C})
, by standard approximation results we get the identity

(B.8)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}d(\bar{u}du)=d\bar{u}\wedge du=2i(du_{1}\wedge du_{2})&\mbox{ distributionally on }\mathbb{S}^{4}\\
d\lvert u\rvert^{2}=2u_{1}du_{1}+2u_{2}du_{2}&\mbox{ in }L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4}).\end{cases}

Notice that (
B.8
) immediately implies that
\bar{u}du
is always a purely imaginary
1
-form on
\mathbb{S}^{4}
. Since
g=g_{1}+ig_{2}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},U(1))\subset W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},\mathbb{C})
, by using (
B.8
) and the constraint
\lvert g\rvert^{2}\equiv 1
, we get

\displaystyle 0=g_{1}dg_{1}+g_{2}dg_{2}=2(dg_{1}\wedge dg_{2})=d(\bar{g}dg)\quad\mbox{ distributionally on }\mathbb{S}^{4}.

Hence,
\bar{g}dg
is a purely imaginary closed
1
-form on
\mathbb{S}^{4}
. Since
H_{dR}^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{4})=0
, standard
L^{2}
-Hodge decomposition on
\mathbb{S}^{4}
(see for instance ) gives the existence of
\varphi\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},\mathbb{R})
such that

\displaystyle id\varphi=\bar{g}dg.

Vol’pert chain rule in
W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},\mathbb{R})
gives

(B.9)

\displaystyle d(e^{-i\varphi})=-ie^{-i\varphi}d\varphi=-e^{-i\varphi}\bar{g}dg\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},U(1)).

By Leibniz rule in the algebra
(L^{\infty}\cap W^{1,2})(\mathbb{S}^{4},\mathbb{C})
and by (
B.9
) we get

\displaystyle d(e^{-i\varphi}g)=d(e^{-i\varphi})g+e^{-i\varphi}dg=-e^{-i\varphi}\bar{g}dgg+e^{-i\varphi}dg=-e^{-i\varphi}\lvert g\rvert^{2}dg+e^{-i\varphi}dg=0,

where in the last equality we have used that
\lvert g\rvert^{2}\equiv 1
. Hence, there exists a constant
g_{0}\in U(1)
such that

\displaystyle g=g_{0}e^{i\varphi}\quad\mbox{ a.e. on }\mathbb{S}^{4}.

Now let
\tilde{\varphi}\in W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5},\mathbb{C})
. be the standard harmonic extension of
\varphi
, i.e. the solution of the following PDE

(B.10)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta\tilde{\varphi}=0&\mbox{ in }\mathbb{B}^{5}\\
\tilde{\varphi}=\varphi&\mbox{ on }\mathbb{S}^{4}.\end{cases}

Let
M_{U(1)}=\{p\}
and
g_{p}:=g_{0}e^{i\tilde{\varphi}}
. By construction, (
B.1
) and (
B.3
) are satisfied. Moreover, by standard trace theory for Sobolev functions
W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5},\mathbb{C})
, the estimates (
B.4
), (
B.6
) and hence the statement follow immediately.
∎

We now tackle the case of a general simply-connected compact Lie group
G
. Recall the following lemma, whose prove can be found in
[
14
, Lemma 6.1]
.

Lemma B.3
.

Let
N\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
be an
n
-dimensional smooth submanifold of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
such that

\displaystyle\pi_{0}(N)=\pi_{1}(N)=\pi_{2}(N)=0.

Then, there exists a compact
(k-4)
-dimensional Lipschitz polyhedron
X\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
such that a locally Lipschitz retraction
Q:\mathbb{R}^{k}\smallsetminus X\to N
such that

(B.11)

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}(0)}\lvert dQ\rvert^{p}\,d\mathcal{L}^{k}<+\infty,

for every
p\in(1,4)
and
r\in(0,+\infty)
. Moreover, the projection map
Q
is smooth of constant rank
n
near the manifold
G\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
and coincides with the identity on
N
.

Lastly, we use Lemma
B.3
to prove the following.

Proposition B.3
.

Proposition
B.1
holds if
G
is a simply connected, compact Lie group.

Proof.

Notice that
\pi_{2}(G)=0
for every compact Lie group and moreover, by Nash embedding theorem, there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
G
is isometrically embedded in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
. Hence, without losing generality, we can assume that
G\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
is a smooth submanifold of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
satisfying the assumptions of Lemma
B.3
. We denote by
Q:\mathbb{R}^{k}\smallsetminus X\to G
the projection map given by applying Lemma
B.3
to
G
. Recall that both
G
and
X
are compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
and let
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
be any open ball in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
containing
G\cup X
. Exactly as in the proof of
[
14
, Theorem 6.2]
, for a small positive number
\sigma\in(0,\operatorname{dist}(G,\partial B))
chosen so that
Q
is smooth on
G+B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)
and any arbitrary point
p\in B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
we define
X_{p}:=X+p=\{y+a\mbox{ : }y\in X\}
and the translated projection
Q_{p}:\mathbb{R}^{k}\smallsetminus X_{a}\to G
given by

(B.12)

\displaystyle Q_{p}(y):=Q(y-p)\qquad\forall\,y\in\mathbb{R}^{k}\smallsetminus X_{p}.

For such a sufficiently small
\sigma
,

\displaystyle\Lambda:=\sup_{p\in B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)}\operatorname{Lip}(Q_{p}|_{G})^{-1}

is, by the inverse function theorem, a finite number depending only on
G
.

Fix any
g\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
and let
\tilde{g}\in W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5},\mathbb{R}^{k})
be the solution of the following PDE:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta\tilde{g}=0&\mbox{ in }\mathbb{B}^{5},\\
\tilde{g}=g&\mbox{ on }\mathbb{S}^{4}.\end{cases}

Notice that, by standard elliptic regularity,
\tilde{g}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{5},\mathbb{R}^{k})
and it satisfies the estimate

(B.13)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{g}-g_{0}\|_{W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\|g-g_{0}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})},

where
g_{0}\in G
is any constant in
G
. Define

\displaystyle\bar{g}:=\fint_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}g\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

and notice that, for every
C^{2}
-domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
the trace theorem for
C^{2}
-domains (see
[
1
, Chapter 1, Section 5.1]
) (
B.13
) with
g_{0}=\bar{g}
and Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality give the estimate

(B.14)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert\iota_{\partial\Omega}^{*}d\tilde{g}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert\iota_{\partial\Omega}^{*}d(\tilde{g}-\bar{g})\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}

(B.15)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|\tilde{g}-\bar{g}\|_{W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}^{2}\leqslant C\|g-\bar{g}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2}\leqslant C(\Omega)\|dg\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}^{2},

for a constant
C(\Omega)>0
depending only on the
C^{2}
-norm of the domain
\Omega
. We have also obviously using classical Sobolev embeddings

(B.16)

\|d\tilde{g}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\,\|\tilde{g}-\overline{g}\|_{W^{\frac{3}{2},2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\,\|dg\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}.

For every
p\in B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)
, we define

g_{p}:=Q_{p}\circ\tilde{g}:\mathbb{B}^{5}\to G

and we notice that, by the area formula and chain rules in Sobolev Spaces
g_{p}\in W^{1,\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{B}^{5},G)
for
\mathcal{L}^{k}
-a.e.
p\in B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)
moreover there holds
g_{p}|_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}=g
for for
\mathcal{L}^{k}
-a.e.
p\in B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)
. Using the fact that by the maximum principle
\|\tilde{g}\|_{\infty}\leqslant\|g\|_{\infty}\leqslant\mbox{diam}(G)
, we have

(B.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{p\in B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|dg_{p}|^{\frac{5}{2}}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}\ d\mathcal{L}^{k}(p)&\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|d\tilde{g}(x)|^{\frac{5}{2}}\ \int_{B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)}|dQ(\tilde{g}(x)-p)|^{\frac{5}{2}}\ d\mathcal{L}^{k}(p)\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}(x)\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|d\tilde{g}(x)|^{\frac{5}{2}}\ \int_{B_{\sigma+\|g\|_{\infty}}^{k}(0)}|dQ(y)|^{\frac{5}{2}}\ d\mathcal{L}^{k}(y)\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}(x)\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C_{G}\int_{\mathbb{B}^{5}}|d\tilde{g}(x)|^{\frac{5}{2}}\ d\mathcal{L}^{5}(x)\\[0.0pt]
&\leqslant C_{G}\,\|dg\|^{\frac{5}{2}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}.\end{split}

Given now any
C^{2}
-domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{5}
, by using Fubini’s theorem, the chain rule and (
B.14
) we infer that

(B.18)

\begin{array}[]{rl}\displaystyle\int_{B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)}\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert\iota_{\partial\Omega}^{*}dg_{p}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\,d\mathcal{L}^{k}&\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert\iota_{\partial\Omega}^{*}d\tilde{g}(x)\rvert^{2}\int_{B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)}\lvert dQ_{p}(\tilde{g}(x))\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{k}(p)\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}(x)\\[0.0pt]
&\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert\iota_{\partial\Omega}^{*}d\tilde{g}(x)\rvert^{2}\int_{B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)}\lvert dQ(\tilde{g}(x)-p)\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{k}(p)\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}(x)\\[0.0pt]
&\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert\iota_{\partial\Omega}^{*}d\tilde{g}(x)\rvert^{2}\bigg(\int_{B}\lvert dQ(y)\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{k}(y)\bigg)\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}(x)\\[0.0pt]
&\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert\iota_{\partial\Omega}^{*}d\tilde{g}(x)\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\leqslant C(\Omega)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{4}}\lvert dg\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4},\end{array}

and we have shown (
B.4
). It remains to prove the continuity of
\operatorname{Ext}
from
H^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
into
L^{2}(\partial\Omega\times B_{\sigma}^{k}(0))
. Let
g^{1}
and
g^{2}
in
W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^{4},G)
, we first have

(B.19)

\|\tilde{g}^{1}-\tilde{g}^{2}\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\,\|g^{1}-g^{2}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}

We write using again Fubini’s theorem

(B.20)

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\int_{B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)}\bigg(\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert g^{1}_{p}-g^{2}_{p}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)\,d\mathcal{L}^{k}(p)\\[0.0pt]
\quad\quad\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\int_{B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)}\lvert Q(\tilde{g}^{1}(x)-p)-Q(\tilde{g}^{2}(x)-p)\rvert^{2}\,d\mathcal{L}^{k}(p)\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}(x)\\[0.0pt]
\end{array}

Recall the Lusin–Lipschitz inequality

(B.21)

\begin{array}[]{l}\lvert Q(\tilde{g}^{1}(x)-p)-Q(\tilde{g}^{2}(x)-p)\rvert^{2}\\[0.0pt]
\displaystyle\quad\quad\leqslant C\,|\tilde{g}^{1}(x)-\tilde{g}^{2}(x)|^{2}\ \left[M(|dQ|)^{2}(\tilde{g}^{1}(x)-p)+M(|dQ|)^{2}(\tilde{g}^{2}(x)-p)\right]\end{array}

where
M(|dQ|)
denotes the Hardy–Littlewood maximal function of
dQ
. Combining (
B.20
), (
B.21
) and the trace theorem we get

(B.22)

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\int_{B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)}\bigg(\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}\lvert g^{1}_{p}-g^{2}_{p}\rvert^{2}\,d\mathscr{H}^{4}\bigg)\,d\mathcal{L}^{k}(p)\\[0.0pt]
\displaystyle\quad\quad\leqslant C\,\int_{\partial\Omega\cap\mathbb{B}^{5}}|\tilde{g}^{1}(x)-\tilde{g}^{2}(x)|^{2}\ \,d\mathscr{H}^{4}(x)\ \|dQ\|_{L^{2}(B_{\sigma}^{k}(0))}^{2}\\[0.0pt]
\displaystyle\quad\quad\leqslant C\ \|\tilde{g}^{1}-\tilde{g}^{2}\|^{2}_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb{B}^{5})}\leqslant C\|g^{1}-g^{2}\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{S}^{4})}.\end{array}

Hence, the statement follows by letting
M_{G}:=B_{\sigma}^{k}(0)
.
∎
